===== arc --help =====
Persistent context and guarded workflow state over plain Git for agentic coding arcs.

Reconstruct project and workflow context across sessions and harnesses: work in progress, decisions, next steps, and what is safe to integrate. Git owns content and history; arc owns the journal, change ledger, review findings, verification evidence, and guarded integration.

Usage: arc [OPTIONS] [COMMAND]

Commands:
  begin           Open a change: create (or adopt) its branch and record its checkout
  roles           Record explicit planning and orchestration assertions on an open change. Values follow arc-contribution-trailers/1; ancestry grants no credit
  trailers        Print a change's portable trailer block in convention order. Planning and orchestration come from assertions, implementation from declared patchset contributors, or the snapshot subject when none are listed. A subject supplies harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>; recorder harness and model evidence applies only to that recorder's contribution. Unresolved contributor coordinates are omitted and named in notes. Review credit stays in the ledger; Reviewed-by is never projected
  list            List changes Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output
  query           Filter changes and print matching IDs (or JSON) Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output
  show            Render one change (Markdown, or full state with --json) Closed head drift compares with the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise with the newest snapshot Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails
  explain         What one change knew and what it was accepted on, from the ledger and the journal. Writes nothing: no event, no ref, no journal entry, no lock
  log             Print the change's recorded facts one line each, in ledger order. A review batch records several, so it renders as several lines. This is the ledger, not Git history: for commits, use `git log`. Model sources, observation coordinates, and declaration disagreements accompany each fact
  stats           Derived ledger analytics: stage, review, and gate durations, and how often provenance is recorded Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  diff            Render a recorded patchset using Git's native diff output Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  findings        List findings in text, JSON, or SARIF 2.1.0 form
  brief           Record or read a change-scoped implementation contract
  changelog       Record, read, or project changelog entries
  message         Append a structured cross-change announcement (never policy input)
  messages        Scan messages across open and closed changes (newest first) Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails
  inbox           Lead-facing queue rollup: open changes, active claims, and outstanding debt (arc-inbox/10 schema) Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails
  chain           Show a tagged program in dependency order (arc-chain/4 schema) Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  take            Atomically claim the highest-priority ready change
  metadata        Append dependency, tag, or assignment metadata to an open change
  iterating       Declare that this change is being iterated on, or clear the declaration
  status          Machine-readable status report (the versioned arc-status/27 schema). Every verdict names its recorded origin, actor claims and admission provenance. Content coverage is separate from receiver gating eligibility. Readiness uses the integration shape resolved for the recorded target. Use check --shape to evaluate an explicit alternative. comparison_patchset names the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise the newest snapshot; head_matches_comparison_patchset compares its head. head_matches_latest_patchset always compares the newest snapshot. An unreadable selected change fails; unreadable neighboring changes are named with their errors on stderr and omitted from the dependency observation. An unreadable prerequisite cannot count as integrated Includes retained provisional deliveries, routing recipients, local merges and dispositions. Unfinished transitions include observed refs, event state, retained intent and recovery command
  blocker-status  Report whether declared prerequisite changes have integrated Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails
  is-blocked      Dependency probe: exit 0 ready, 1 blocked, 2 on lookup/ledger errors Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails
  events          Replay raw ledger events as NDJSON, optionally following new events Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  watch           Wait for a change, or a tagged series, to reach a ledger-derived condition Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  export          Export one change as a deterministic, versioned JSON bundle. Exporter/project metadata is distinct from the preserved event origins. Includes a role-labelled Git object list derived from the exported events, including a delta's suffix and repository events. The summary counts objects by role; transports must deliver them before import. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  import          Import arc-bundle/6 or /5 into this repository's local store. Received-byte digest, object availability, and publishing authorization are checked before publication; either refusal writes nothing. Unless --provenance-only is selected, every listed Git object must exist locally before any events or refs are written. Availability checks never fetch from a promisor remote. Bundles without an objects field retain advisory commit checks. Paired stores and receivers with enrollments require a transport-asserted publisher and exact received-byte SHA256. Operator enrollment authorizes each introduced origin and capability; actor strings remain claims. Admission and its private authorization receipt publish atomically. Receipt permissions bind full canonical content and scope. Enrollment is revalidated under the publication lock; unpublished failures leave the format stamp unchanged. --provenance-only permits missing objects; it never bypasses authorization. Candidate events stamp store format 7, including evaluations, selections, and promotions without a registration in the bundle. Events without model provenance retain that absence. Repository map withdrawals apply even to maps already held; invalid withdrawal targets are refused before anything is written. So is a change history no command could have written: work after the change closed, an audit or post-integration disposition before it integrated, or a post-integration disposition on a finding that was resolved, accepted-risk, or obsolete when the change shipped
  accept          Prepare a delivered change on the paired integration authority (lead only). Admission precedes local checkout binding, exclusive scoped signing, snapshotting at the delivered base and bound brief, local gates, and baseline/final probes. Execution or observation errors remain distinct from failed gates and probe discrimination. Preparation records evidence and returns readiness; it records no judgment. A local review still owed normally exits 3. Moved authoritative history requires explicit rebase, snapshot, and review. Stops at the first failure and reports refs, objects, events and pending rewrite recovery state, including effects preceding event publication. Unpublished bindings report administrative ownership and allocation proof; retry can finish a locally owned, empty, index-free checkout after guarded identity checks. Unrelated contents or changed ownership refuse recovery. Commands carry executable argv/cwd; internal bindings and signature checks are identified separately. Local event IDs follow admitted history even when the sender's clock is ahead. Retrying the same delivery reuses admission, checkout binding, signature recovery, and snapshot. Dry-run validates admission and local state without allocating, signing, executing checks, creating objects, or recording evidence; future outcomes are conditional. JSON emits one arc-accept/1 transcript
  replica         Pair independent stores and move integration authority with files. Checkout paths and ownership belong to the recording replica. Local checkout bindings take precedence; relayed bindings grant no cleanup rights
  check           Integration preflight under the resolved target shape or --shape; exit code identifies the first blocker Missing asserted contribution trailers are advisory only. The remedy is arc rewrite trailers --from <rev> --append <line> before integration Integration preflight under the resolved target shape or --shape; exit code identifies the first blocker. arc-check/4 reports recorded origins, admission provenance, content coverage, gating eligibility, and the selected authorizing verdict
  claim           Acquire or renew an advisory executor claim on a change or a journal artifact. Journal artifact claims require a declared actor (--actor/ARC_ACTOR), harness (--harness/ARC_HARNESS), and session (--session/ARC_SESSION)
  release-claim   Release the advisory executor claim on a change or a journal artifact
  stage           Record typed executor progress (requires an owned live claim)
  squash          Rewrite a change as one commit on its base, with the same tree, and record it as a new patchset. Evidence and verdicts on the earlier heads stay with those heads; the single commit is gated and reviewed like any other patchset. A failed commit or hook-modified tree restores the original head, index, and tracked files; untracked files are retained. Obstructing paths are moved to a reported recovery directory under <git-common-dir>/arc/squash-recovery/. Over another actor's live claim, a squash without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the patchset is recorded, so a command acting on the change while the commit is made reports the lock busy
  snapshot        Record the current branch head as a patchset with explicit base and brief binding. Accept preserves the delivered binding and contributor declaration through signature-only preparation; snapshots record evidence, never a judgment
  keep            Keep a fact this work discovered, so `arc resume` hands it back to a compacted or cold session instead of it being re-derived
  comment         Add a discussion comment
  finding         Record a standalone review finding
  reply           Reply to a comment or finding event
  resolve         Record a finding disposition (supersedes current tips automatically)
  review          Read review state, or record a verdict with an optional findings batch. Recorded history includes imported inputs; receiver eligibility requires admitted review-authority and operator trust for a foreign origin. Foreign verdicts cannot replace the receiver's local judgment
  external        Record a review decision made outside the local ledger
  verify          Run a declared gate (or ad hoc command) and record the evidence. Gate evidence only counts at the change's own head, so the command runs in the change's recorded worktree whichever checkout it was typed in; `--attest` records evidence arc did not run. Acceptance executes local head gates even when imported evidence passed, and selects probes from the delivered patchset's bound brief. Baselines run in scratch checkouts
  done            Finish implementation: snapshot, verify all gates, then print check state. A profile with no declared gate records no evidence and prints that no gate is declared instead of a pass
  rebase          Replay a change's branch onto its target, then snapshot the new head. The snapshot links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file; a file named by both is linked once as `begin`. An unavailable artifact is omitted with a warning on stderr. Over another actor's live claim, a rebase without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the replayed head is recorded, so a command acting on the change during the replay reports the lock busy. The gates the replayed head owes are named after it is recorded; a failure to evaluate them is a warning, and the rebase still succeeds
  env             Print shell exports for a detected harness session, for `eval`: `eval "$(arc env)"`
  completions     Print a shell completion script to stdout Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  mangen          Render the man page (arc.1) into a directory Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  resume          Resume one change with its brief, live state, and journal context Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  rescue          Recover work abandoned by another session
  prompt          Print one stable statusline summary for the current change Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  fork            Fork this repository: a worktree on a fork/<slug> branch, outside the change lifecycle. Unintegrated by intent; the operator decides what to merge, rebase, or discard
  hold            Set an integration hold
  release-hold    Release one hold by the event that set it (a unique prefix is enough)
  integrate       Explicit --provisional prepares one unsigned no-ff merge into an unchecked staging ref under refs/heads/arc/provisional/<local-replica>/<recorded-target>. Canonical policy, review and gate requirements apply. Publication and closure remain owed to the recipient, defaulting to the known authority holder. Recipient flags route an obligation and grant no authority. Provisional prerequisites satisfy local preparation only; authority publication requires canonical closures. Replacing provisional ancestry requires a content replay, a new patchset and fresh review or explicit debt. Guarded integration of one change, or of a dependency-ordered queue named by several changes or by --tag
  debt            Record a review obligation this change carries but has not discharged
  audit           Record a review performed after integration (never a late verdict)
  close           Close a change without arc performing the merge
  history         Record or withdraw rewrite maps, and resolve recorded revisions
  rewrite         Rewrite this repository's history, carrying every recorded revision forward
  candidate       Register, show, list, judge, and retire candidates: alternative answers to a brief, recorded without opening a change
  context         Record what a change or a candidate read, declared, and captured: read records from a tool's record, attributed declarations, and capture reports
  pass            Record and list caller-declared review passes
  run             Record delegated run dispatches and their terminal outcomes
  policy          Read or write policy kept with this repository's local Arc ledger
  forge           Record and validate observed forge (hosted-PR) facts
  config          Show resolved paths and style values with sources as arc-config/1 JSON Style precedence: flag, operator defaults, HEAD project defaults, user, builtin. Style inspection parses user configuration and repository policy; malformed style or constraint tables and known keys refuse with file and key. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  sandbox         Make, compare, and remove a disposable copy of this project under a sandbox prefix
  doctor          Check the append-only ledger for malformed or stale state (read-only) Reports the build commit and tracked-tree clean/dirty state in text and arc-doctor/6 JSON; builds outside Git report unknown. Unfinished provisional transitions report their merge, refs, event state, retained intent and exact recovery command as problems
  hooks           Manage the opt-in Git hook pack (never installed automatically)
  instructions    Print a portable convention specification, independent of arc state
  workspace       Aggregate changes, inboxes, or backlog across every known project
  restack         Advise (never execute) rebases for open dependents of a change Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  catchup         Orient a session: ledger queue, journal backlog, live lanes, and recent sessions across harnesses. Recent activity without filed artifacts or a naming handoff/conclusion is marked unfiled. Filing counts include direct writes and published transition successors, with import and spool provenance. Stale filings name the `journal filed` query; plain reads record no activity. Project memories precede global memory titles with their ages in days; bodies are read on demand with journal show --global. An unavailable journal carries its own warning, including global_memory_error or project_journal_error in JSON; readable sources remain in the output. Each recording backend is queried once with an unrestricted candidate window. Backend scan bounds and failures appear in session_discovery_errors; readable rows remain ranked by recorded activity. Pending transitions carry a diagnostic and do not count as filings or naming handoffs/conclusions. Text warns when the running build differs from HEAD in an arc-ledger checkout: older for an ancestor, differs otherwise, with dirty builds identified. Other projects and unknown build revisions have no warning. Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output Provisional integration obligations have a separate section and do not count as review debt
  fr              File a feature request in the project journal: the discoverable alias for `arc journal note --kind feature-request`. Read the queue back with `arc journal open` or `arc journal list --kind feature-request`
  journal         Cross-harness project and operator journals over plain Markdown. Project reads also resolve from their journal directory; project writes name the checkout they need. --global works from any directory
  help            Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version

Run `arc` with no arguments for the workflow guide, or `arc catchup` for live project state.

===== arc begin --help =====
Open a change: create (or adopt) its branch and record its checkout

New branches use [change] checkout (built-in: in-place), overridden by --worktree or --no-worktree. --adopt records the branch's existing worktree; it switches this checkout only with explicit --no-worktree and never creates a worktree.

Worktree ownership binds to the allocated Git administrative directory before checkout hooks run. If the registered path changes identity, the change opens without creation authority and all worktrees are kept. Worktree post-checkout receives Git's creation arguments (null old revision, HEAD, 1). Population or hook failure retains the branch and worktree, removes the unrecorded owner token when its identity matches, and reports adoption and discard commands without opening a change. Adoption recovery preserves the opening options and resolved target and base, attaching option values as --flag=<shell-quoted value> to preserve leading hyphens. Checkout selection follows the retained branch.

Without --base, the change derives its base from the local target: its head, or under --adopt the adopted branch's merge base with it. When that target lacks commits its configured upstream holds, as the last fetch left the remote-tracking ref (arc reads local refs only, never the network), the change still opens and stderr warns, naming both revisions, the base the change took, and the fix: for a target strictly behind, the fast-forward (`git merge --ff-only` in the checkout holding it, or `git fetch . <upstream>:<target>` when none does); for a diverged one, that no fast-forward exists; then `arc rebase` onto the moved target. A target level with or ahead of its upstream, or tracking none, gets no warning.

Usage: arc begin [OPTIONS] <SLUG>

Arguments:
  <SLUG>
          Kebab-case slug naming the outcome (also the ID prefix)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --planned-by [<PLANNED_BY>]
          Assert a planner: harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>; repeatable. Without a value, use the declared invoking identity; unresolved coordinates refuse

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --orchestrated-by [<ORCHESTRATED_BY>]
          Assert an orchestrator in the same form; repeatable. Without a value, use the declared invoking identity; unresolved coordinates refuse

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --title <TITLE>
          Human title (defaults to the slug with spaces)

      --profile <PROFILE>
          Workflow profile: direct | local | forge | release
          
          [default: local]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --target <TARGET>
          Integration target branch (defaults to the current branch)

      --base <BASE>
          Base revision (defaults to the target head, or under --adopt to the adopted branch's merge base with the target). An explicit base skips the comparison of the target with its upstream

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --branch <BRANCH>
          Branch name (defaults to [change] branch_prefix followed by the slug; built-in arc/)

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --worktree [<WORKTREE>]
          Force a worktree for a new branch, optionally at this path. Without a path, use worktrees_dir/<repo>-<slug>

      --no-worktree
          Switch this checkout to the change branch, carrying local work. Create the branch unless --adopt is supplied. Refuse overwrite conflicts, including ignored files, or a branch checked out elsewhere without opening a change

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --adopt <ADOPT>
          Track an existing branch instead of creating one

      --blocked-by <BLOCKED_BY>
          Change that must integrate before this one is ready (repeatable)

      --tag <TAG>
          Batch/query tag (repeatable)

      --from-journal <FROM_JOURNAL>
          Open from an actionable journal artifact, consuming it. A bare filename resolves in this project's journal; `<journal-dir>::<file>` resolves `<file>` in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` that project's `arc journal dir` prints, and records the promotion in that journal, naming this repository and change. A journal that is not one, or a file it does not hold, is refused naming both; a filename this project's journal does not hold, naming each other known journal that holds it; a path, naming the reference that resolves it. The change records the reference as given and the digest of its body read now, and each later snapshot links it `via: begin` unless given --journal-ref

      --from-fork <FROM_FORK>
          Open by promoting a fork's work onto a new branch, recording the fork slug and the source base, head, and tree. Recovery adoption requires an explicit --base. The fork keeps its branch, worktree, and marker; no review credit crosses the link

      --dangerous
          Require an independent verdict whatever this change turns out to touch. One-way: nothing lowers it afterwards

      --iterating
          Open the change declaring that integration is not yet the goal

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc roles --help =====
Record explicit planning and orchestration assertions on an open change. Values follow arc-contribution-trailers/1; ancestry grants no credit

Usage: arc roles [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>  Open change whose contribution assertions to record

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --planned-by <PLANNED_BY>
          Assert a planner (repeatable harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>)
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --orchestrated-by <ORCHESTRATED_BY>
          Assert an orchestrator (repeatable harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>)
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --withdraw <WITHDRAW>
          Withdraw an exact assertion (repeatable Planned-by=VALUE or Orchestrated-by=VALUE)
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help
          Print help

===== arc trailers --help =====
Print a change's portable trailer block in convention order. Planning and orchestration come from assertions, implementation from declared patchset contributors, or the snapshot subject when none are listed. A subject supplies harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>; recorder harness and model evidence applies only to that recorder's contribution. Unresolved contributor coordinates are omitted and named in notes. Review credit stays in the ledger; Reviewed-by is never projected

Usage: arc trailers [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>  Change whose attributed contribution trailers to print

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --json                         Emit arc-trailers/1 JSON with trailers and attribution notes
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc list --help =====
List changes Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output

Usage: arc list [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --open                         List only changes that are still open
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
      --format <FORMAT>              [default: default] [possible values: default, wide, compact, json]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc query --help =====
Filter changes and print matching IDs (or JSON) Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output

Usage: arc query [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --status <STATUS>              open | closed | integrated | abandoned | superseded
      --target <TARGET>              Only changes whose integration target is this branch
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --tag <TAG>                    Only changes carrying every tag given (repeatable)
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --verdict <VERDICT>            Only changes whose latest verdict is this [possible values: approved, changes-requested, comment-only]
      --actor <ACTOR>                Only changes opened by this actor
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --harness <HARNESS>            Only changes opened from this harness
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --commit <COMMIT>              Report changes whose patchset, integration, or closure commit matches this revision (unique prefix accepted)
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --debt                         Only changes that integrated owing a review nobody has recorded yet
      --provisional                  Only changes whose gating approval was recorded as owed corroboration and has not received it — from an independent approval of the same patchset, or from an audit
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc explain --help =====
What one change knew and what it was accepted on, from the ledger and the journal. Writes nothing: no event, no ref, no journal entry, no lock.

Eight slots, always all printed: contract (the brief in force, its planner credit, plan, base revision, and causes, and the selection and promotion behind each patchset a candidate promotion recorded), supplied context (the journal artifact the change was opened from, and every patchset's journal references with the operation that supplied each, `begin`, `brief`, `flag`, or `unrecorded`, and its thread), declared facts (every kept fact, with the events it cites), observed reads, rejected alternatives (kept facts of kind `rejected`, and each registration answering the same brief version as a promoted candidate, `declared` with its judgements or `inferred` while unjudged; judgements are read as they stand now, whatever `--at` names), evaluation (each counted gate's evidence event, tree, environment digest, timeout, reuse, and falsification), coverage at acceptance (the verdicts, waiver, and debt the integration event's authorization recorded), and later knowledge (audits, audit findings and dispositions, debts, and debt discharges recorded after the integration).

Every row carries a standing: `recorded` (an event records it), `declared` (somebody stated it and arc did not check it), `inferred` (arc derived it from other records; no event states it), `absent` (nothing records it), or `unavailable` (its source cannot be read now). Every standing but `recorded` gives its reason, and an empty slot prints its absence and why.

A journal reference is resolved against the journal now, hot or cold: `same` when the body digest equals the recorded one, `amended` with both digests when not, `missing` when the name resolves to nothing. The artifact a change was opened from resolves the same way against the digest its opening recorded, and is `recorded`; a change opened before arc recorded that digest keeps it `declared`, with its digest shown as `current`. A kept fact's citations name records; the fact stays `declared`.

A counted pass's falsification lists a declared one (with its predicted reason) and the failure arc inferred it follows (standing `inferred`, with the failing event, its revision, and the rule that derived it) as separate entries, or `none` when neither was recorded.

A discharge of a debt by a later review that is not an approval reads `fulfilled, not approved`. Coverage at acceptance never takes later knowledge into its values; it only points at the discharge.

Usage: arc explain [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --at <AT>
          Bound the view by this event ID on the change. For an integrated change, supplied context, declared facts, rejected alternatives, and later knowledge show only what was recorded by the event, while contract, evaluation, and coverage at acceptance keep their integration basis; the integration event itself, or any earlier one, leaves later knowledge empty. For a change not integrated it replays the change as of the event, as `arc show --at` does. An event not on the change is refused

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --json
          Emit `arc-explain/1` JSON instead of text

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc brief --help =====
Record or read a change-scoped implementation contract

Probe edits without --body-file or --scaffold require a preceding brief and preserve its body and omitted title, plan provenance, and mandatory reads. Explicit metadata replaces the corresponding fields. A body or scaffold records a replacement contract.

Usage: arc brief [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --planned-by <PLANNED_BY>
          Assert a planner on the change when writing a brief (repeatable). Value: harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read a new brief body from a file ('-' for stdin). A write records the next version: v1 on a change with no brief, otherwise one past the latest

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --title <TITLE>
          Optional title for a newly recorded brief

      --base <BASE>
          Revision whose source and premises this brief was checked against. Defaults to the previous brief's base, or HEAD for the first brief

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --version <VERSION>
          Read one derived brief version instead of the latest

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>
          Scaffold template around the body: .arc/templates/<name>.md, or a built-in (sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one

      --plan-ref <PLAN_REF>
          Journal plan artifact implemented by this brief: a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for a plan in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The brief records the reference as given; a plan in another journal is read there, and that journal records the brief as a promotion naming this repository and change. A filename this project's journal does not hold is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path, naming the reference that resolves it

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --plan-slice <PLAN_SLICE>
          Opaque plan slice slug implemented by this brief

      --probes-json <PROBES_JSON>
          Named acceptance probes bound to this brief: a JSON array inline, a path to one, or '-' for stdin. Replaces the complete probe array; without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --probe <PROBE>
          Add or update a named acceptance probe (repeatable NAME=COMMAND). Other probes carry forward from the previous brief. Mutually exclusive with --probes-json. Without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --withdraw-probe <WITHDRAW_PROBE>
          Withdraw a named probe (repeatable); other probes carry forward. Unknown names are refused. Mutually exclusive with --probes-json. Without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --caused-by <CAUSED_BY>
          Earlier ledger fact that caused this version (repeatable): finding:<id>, verdict:<event> naming a changes-requested verdict, or blocked-on:<event>, each on this change. v1 refuses a cause; every later version requires at least one, from this flag or --cause-note

      --cause-note <CAUSE_NOTE>
          External cause, when no earlier ledger object represents the reason. It is a cause like --caused-by: refused on v1, and enough alone for any later version

      --must-read <MUST_READ>
          A read the contract requires of whoever answers it (repeatable): a journal artifact, `<file>` or `<journal-dir>::<file>`, at `@sha256:<hex>` or at the body digest read now; or a file at a revision, `<revision>:<path>[:<from>-<to>]`, recorded as its blob and the one-based inclusive line range, whole when none is given. A locator that resolves to nothing is refused, an artifact filename this project's journal does not hold naming each other known journal that holds it, and a path to an artifact naming the reference that resolves it. `arc candidate select` meets a requirement only with a tool read at that version covering that extent. An artifact requirement is met by digest equality with the text a tool returned, so a tool that decorates what it returns, like a line-numbered `Read`, never meets one

      --json
          Emit the versioned structured brief projection

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc changelog --help =====
Record, read, or project changelog entries

While an open change has no changelog record, `arc status` and `arc check` advise one under the code `no-changelog-entry`, and `arc integrate` prints that advice once it has merged such a change; the advice never blocks. Recording an entry, or recording with --none that the change needs none, answers it. The latest record wins, and a --none record projects nothing.

The projection is rendered by the renderer `.arc/changelog.toml` selects: the built-in `keep-a-changelog`, or `command`, whose `renderer_command` argv runs from the repository root without a shell, with the authority of whoever runs arc changelog. It reads an arc-changelog-render-request/1 document on stdin and answers on stdout; `renderer_timeout` bounds it (default 60s), and its process group is killed at the deadline. `--json` never runs it.

Read-only projections name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable entries. --write requires every change to be readable; an unreadable selected change fails

Usage: arc changelog [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change whose entry is recorded or read. Omitted, recording infers it from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in, while a read projects every change's entry instead

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --category <CATEGORY>
          Free-form category for a newly recorded changelog entry

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read a new entry body from a file ('-' for stdin)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --none
          Record that the change needs no changelog entry, replacing any entry recorded before. Requires --reason

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --reason <REASON>
          Why the change needs no changelog entry; only with --none

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --json
          Emit a read result as JSON

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --provenance
          Include recording identity in human-readable output

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --since <SINCE>
          Override the latest-tag release boundary

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --write
          Write the configured target. The built-in renderer replaces the generated [Unreleased] block in CHANGELOG.md; each paragraph and list item, bulleted or numbered, of an entry is refilled to 75 columns, continuations indented under their marker and never begun with a word Markdown would read as opening a block, and a fenced or indented code block keeps its lines, and empty list items keep their markers. Every line keeps its column within the list item holding it; where the entry's own bullet indents a line, a tab in front of its text is written as the spaces it stood for. The block is judged paragraph by paragraph, on words rather than the column they are wrapped at, and refused, naming each paragraph, while it holds prose no recorded entry produced. The block runs to the next release heading or the end of the file; a missing target, or one with no [Unreleased] heading, is refused with exit 1 and nothing written. A command renderer's stdout replaces the whole target, atomically, only after it exits 0 with a non-empty answer

      --keep-unrecorded
          Keep the block's unrecorded paragraphs whole above the projected entries, under an unrecorded marker, instead of refusing to write. Built-in renderer only

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc message --help =====
Append a structured cross-change announcement (never policy input)

Usage: arc message [OPTIONS] --type <MESSAGE_TYPE> --summary <SUMMARY> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>  Change the announcement is recorded against

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --type <MESSAGE_TYPE>          Announcement class [possible values: status, discovery, note]
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --summary <SUMMARY>            Required single-line summary
      --detail <DETAIL>              Optional longer detail

      --severity <SEVERITY>          Advisory severity [default: info] [possible values: info, warning, error]

===== arc status --help =====
Machine-readable status report (the versioned arc-status/27 schema). Every verdict names its recorded origin, actor claims and admission provenance. Content coverage is separate from receiver gating eligibility. Readiness uses the integration shape resolved for the recorded target. Use check --shape to evaluate an explicit alternative. comparison_patchset names the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise the newest snapshot; head_matches_comparison_patchset compares its head. head_matches_latest_patchset always compares the newest snapshot. An unreadable selected change fails; unreadable neighboring changes are named with their errors on stderr and omitted from the dependency observation. An unreadable prerequisite cannot count as integrated Includes retained provisional deliveries, routing recipients, local merges and dispositions. Unfinished transitions include observed refs, event state, retained intent and recovery command

Usage: arc status [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --json                         Accepted for compatibility; status output is always JSON
      --get <GET>                    Print one dotted object-key/array-index path
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --fields <FIELDS>              Print a top-level JSON field subset
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --at <AT>                      Replay state as of this event ID ("what did the actor see?")
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc watch --help =====
Wait for a change, or a tagged series, to reach a ledger-derived condition Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc watch [OPTIONS] --until <UNTIL> [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          A bare `.md` filename held only by another known journal is refused, naming its owning journal and project. Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --tag <TAG>
          Watch every change carrying all supplied tags (repeatable)

      --any
          With --tag: return when any one member reaches a condition

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --all
          With --tag: return when every member has reached a condition

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --until <UNTIL>
          Condition to wait for (repeatable, comma-separated): `snapshot`, `stalled`, `reviewed`, `approved`, `gates-green`, `ready`, `blocked`, `brief-recorded`, `integrated`, or `closed`.
          
          `stalled` is a stage clock, not an activity check: it holds once the change's live claim has sat in one stage longer than that stage's budget (`arc claim --stage-budget`), counted from its last `arc stage`, or from the claim while it is still at `launch`. Only `arc stage`, re-reporting the current stage included, or a snapshot under the claim restarts the clock; output, logs, and claim renewals do not. The reached line and the JSON name the stage, its age, and its budget. On a journal artifact, whose claim takes stages but no stage budgets, `stalled` means the claim's lease ran out.
          
          `reviewed` returns on any verdict against the patchset under review, whatever it concluded, and names the verdict event so the caller can read which. `approved` returns on the latest approving verdict, including a provisional approval and its reason. `gates-green` waits for every required gate to be green at the current head. `blocked` and `brief-recorded` name their events. `ready` is stricter and different: approved, gates green, no blockers — a review asking for changes never satisfies it, so waiting on `ready` for a dispatched review cannot tell a reviewer still working from one that answered.
          
          [possible values: snapshot, stalled, reviewed, approved, gates-green, ready, blocked, brief-recorded, integrated, closed]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --timeout <TIMEOUT>
          Fail with exit 2 after this many seconds

      --exec <EXEC_COMMAND>
          Run `sh -c <cmd>` once when a condition is reached, with the reached `arc-watch/1` document `--json` prints on stdin and ARC_EVENT_TYPE set to `watch-reached`. ARC_EVENT_ID and ARC_CHANGE_ID carry the document's values, empty where it names none

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --json
          Emit the outcome as one `arc-watch/1` JSON object: `schema`, `event_type` (`watch-reached` or `watch-timeout`), and `condition`, the one reached or, on a timeout and under `--all`, every awaited one joined by commas. A reached change adds `change_id`, `event_id` when an event satisfied the condition, `provisional` on a provisional approval, and for `stalled` `stage`, `age_seconds`, and `budget_seconds`. `--all` lists one such member object per change under `changes`. A journal artifact names `file` and `claim_id`

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc export --help =====
Export one change as a deterministic, versioned JSON bundle. Exporter/project metadata is distinct from the preserved event origins. Includes a role-labelled Git object list derived from the exported events, including a delta's suffix and repository events. The summary counts objects by role; transports must deliver them before import. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc export [OPTIONS] --output <OUTPUT> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>  Change to export as a versioned bundle

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --output <OUTPUT>              Output file ('-' for stdout)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --since <SHA256>               Export only the events after this history checksum, which the receiving store must already hold. Repository events travel in every bundle, even when the change suffix is empty
      --objects-json <FILE>          Write an `arc-bundle-objects/1` JSON envelope with `schema` and `objects` fields for a transport
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc import --help =====
Import arc-bundle/6 or /5 into this repository's local store. Received-byte digest, object availability, and publishing authorization are checked before publication; either refusal writes nothing. Unless --provenance-only is selected, every listed Git object must exist locally before any events or refs are written. Availability checks never fetch from a promisor remote. Bundles without an objects field retain advisory commit checks. Paired stores and receivers with enrollments require a transport-asserted publisher and exact received-byte SHA256. Operator enrollment authorizes each introduced origin and capability; actor strings remain claims. Admission and its private authorization receipt publish atomically. Receipt permissions bind full canonical content and scope. Enrollment is revalidated under the publication lock; unpublished failures leave the format stamp unchanged. --provenance-only permits missing objects; it never bypasses authorization. Candidate events stamp store format 7, including evaluations, selections, and promotions without a registration in the bundle. Events without model provenance retain that absence. Repository map withdrawals apply even to maps already held; invalid withdrawal targets are refused before anything is written. So is a change history no command could have written: work after the change closed, an audit or post-integration disposition before it integrated, or a post-integration disposition on a finding that was resolved, accepted-risk, or obsolete when the change shipped

Usage: arc import [OPTIONS] <INPUT>

Arguments:
  <INPUT>  Input file ('-' for stdin)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --publisher <PUBLISHER>
          Publishing principal asserted by the authenticated receiving caller
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256>
          SHA256 of the exact received bytes, supplied by the receiving caller
      --dry-run
          Validate and report without writing files, Git objects, events, or refs
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --provenance-only
          Import events even when listed Git objects are missing, reporting each missing OID and role. Evidence requires those objects to arrive
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help
          Print help

===== arc accept --help =====
Prepare a delivered change on the paired integration authority (lead only). Admission precedes local checkout binding, exclusive scoped signing, snapshotting at the delivered base and bound brief, local gates, and baseline/final probes. Execution or observation errors remain distinct from failed gates and probe discrimination. Preparation records evidence and returns readiness; it records no judgment. A local review still owed normally exits 3. Moved authoritative history requires explicit rebase, snapshot, and review. Stops at the first failure and reports refs, objects, events and pending rewrite recovery state, including effects preceding event publication. Unpublished bindings report administrative ownership and allocation proof; retry can finish a locally owned, empty, index-free checkout after guarded identity checks. Unrelated contents or changed ownership refuse recovery. Commands carry executable argv/cwd; internal bindings and signature checks are identified separately. Local event IDs follow admitted history even when the sender's clock is ahead. Retrying the same delivery reuses admission, checkout binding, signature recovery, and snapshot. Dry-run validates admission and local state without allocating, signing, executing checks, creating objects, or recording evidence; future outcomes are conditional. JSON emits one arc-accept/1 transcript

Usage: arc accept [OPTIONS] --branch <BRANCH> --publisher <PUBLISHER> --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256> <INPUT>

Arguments:
  <INPUT>  Delivered arc-bundle/6 or /5 file ('-' for stdin)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --branch <BRANCH>
          Existing local branch matching the delivered change branch and head
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --publisher <PUBLISHER>
          Authenticated receiving caller's enrolled publishing principal
      --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256>
          SHA256 of the exact received bytes
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --worktree <WORKTREE>
          Matching clean checkout, or absent path for an owned allocation
      --key <KEY>
          Signing key override; otherwise use Git's configured key
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --contributors <CONTRIBUTORS>
          Explicit contributor actors; defaults to the delivered declaration
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --solo
          Declare the acting subject as the sole contributor
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --dry-run
          Validate and report conditional steps without effects
      --json

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>

===== arc replica --help =====
Pair independent stores and move integration authority with files. Checkout paths and ownership belong to the recording replica. Local checkout bindings take precedence; relayed bindings grant no cleanup rights

Usage: arc replica [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

  init         Start a logical project and hold its initial integration authority
  pair         Record a peer identity that can adopt the project by importing an export
  export       Export this replica's known identity and authority events. source_replica_id identifies the exporter; event origins remain intact. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  import       Import a pairing record or authority exchange from another replica. Paired stores and enrolled receivers require --publisher and --received-sha256. Pairing grants membership, never publishing permission. Accepts arc-replica-bundle/3 and /2; absent model provenance stays absent

Integration preflight under the resolved target shape or --shape; exit code identifies the first blocker Missing asserted contribution trailers are advisory only. The remedy is arc rewrite trailers --from <rev> --append <line> before integration Integration preflight under the resolved target shape or --shape; exit code identifies the first blocker. arc-check/4 reports recorded origins, admission provenance, content coverage, gating eligibility, and the selected authorizing verdict.

          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Exit status, with blockers in precedence order:
  0   ready to integrate
  17  integration-mode
  6   closed, branch-missing, target-unreadable
  15  fork-branch
  13  iterating
  7   blocked-by-changes
  11  needs-rebase
  14  merged-tree-unevaluated
  2   blocking-findings
  3   no-valid-approval
  5   gates-not-green
  12  acceptance-probes-not-green
  4   hold-active
  1   an error, named on stderr
  2   also a usage error, refused before any change is read
A change with several blockers exits with the first one's code. With --tag, the command exits with the code of the first blocked change it lists, or 0 when every match is ready or closed.

===== arc squash --help =====
Rewrite a change as one commit on its base, with the same tree, and record it as a new patchset. Evidence and verdicts on the earlier heads stay with those heads; the single commit is gated and reviewed like any other patchset. A failed commit or hook-modified tree restores the original head, index, and tracked files; untracked files are retained. Obstructing paths are moved to a reported recovery directory under <git-common-dir>/arc/squash-recovery/. Over another actor's live claim, a squash without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the patchset is recorded, so a command acting on the change while the commit is made reports the lock busy

Usage: arc squash [OPTIONS] --message <MESSAGE> [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
  -m, --message <MESSAGE>
          Message for the single commit
      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
