===== arc snapshot --help =====
Record the current branch head as a patchset with explicit base and brief binding. Accept preserves the delivered binding and contributor declaration through signature-only preparation; snapshots record evidence, never a judgment

Usage: arc snapshot [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --base <BASE>
          Override the recorded base revision
      --brief-version <BRIEF_VERSION>
          Brief version this patchset implements (defaults to latest)
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --verify
          Run verification after recording the patchset
      --gate <GATE>
          Gate name from .arc/gates.toml (repeatable with --verify). Names must be unique and declared; invalid names refuse before any write
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --all
          Run every gate declared for the change profile
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor
      --amend <PATCHSET>
          Amend one patchset's contributors before any verdict exists
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --journal-ref <FILE>
          Journal artifact this patchset was framed by (repeatable): a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for one in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The link records the reference as given. Its body digest is read from the owning journal when the patchset is recorded, and a name that resolves to no artifact is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path is refused naming the reference that resolves it. Given once or more, only the flagged artifacts are linked, `via: flag`. Omitted, the patchset links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file, a file named by both linked once as `begin`; a default that no longer resolves is left out with a warning on stderr. A rerun at an unchanged head with neither this nor --thread keeps existing links
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
      --thread <SCHEME:ID>
          External thread this work belongs to, as SCHEME:ID. Arc stores the identifiers and never fetches or resolves them
  -h, --help
          Print help

===== arc keep --help =====
Keep a fact this work discovered, so `arc resume` hands it back to a compacted or cold session instead of it being re-derived

Usage: arc keep [OPTIONS] --kind <KIND> [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --kind <KIND>                  What kind of fact: a premise checked, an approach abandoned, a constraint discovered, or something believed but not established [possible values: verified, rejected, constraint, hypothesis]
      --body <BODY>                  Inline body text
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Read body from file ('-' for stdin)
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --evidence <EVIDENCE>          What established it. A fact with no evidence reads as a claim
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --cites <EVENT>                An event on this change the fact rests on (repeatable, exact id): a verification or reused verification, a verdict, external verdict, or audit verdict, a finding or audit finding, a disposition or audit disposition, or an earlier kept fact. An id that names no event on this change, or an event of any other kind, is refused and nothing is kept. A citation names the record the fact rests on; it does not make the fact verified, and `--kind` means what it means without one
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc resolve --help =====
Record a finding disposition (supersedes current tips automatically)

A review finding takes its disposition while the change is open. On an integrated change, a review finding left open when the change shipped takes a post-integration disposition, recorded beside the finding state the change shipped with, which stays as it shipped; readers show both. A finding released by the receiver's eligible disposition graph at integration takes none. Recorded dispositions replay independently of receiver trust; eligibility is assessed in the receiver's projection. An audit finding takes its disposition after integration. A disposition is not a verdict and discharges no review debt.

Usage: arc resolve [OPTIONS] --status <STATUS> [CHANGE] <FINDING>

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

  <FINDING>
          The finding being disposed of

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --status <STATUS>
          How the finding stands. resolved, accepted-risk, and obsolete release a blocking finding
          
          [possible values: resolved, accepted-risk, obsolete, still-open, disputed]

      --commit <COMMIT>
          Fixing commit, when one exists. After integration it may be a commit another change landed

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --evidence <EVIDENCE>
          What supports the disposition: a probe, a command, or the reasoning

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --evidence-event <ID>
          The verification event that justifies it: a full event ID on this change, recorded or reused. Prefixes are not resolved, and this neither implies nor is implied by --evidence

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc review --help =====
Read review state, or record a verdict with an optional findings batch. Recorded history includes imported inputs; receiver eligibility requires admitted review-authority and operator trust for a foreign origin. Foreign verdicts cannot replace the receiver's local judgment.

Once a verdict is recorded, review says whether an approval gates. That is advice about a verdict that already stands: a failure to evaluate it is a warning naming the recorded verdict, and the review still succeeds. Verdicts retain observed publication authority. A verified authority refusal on a provisional delivery remains applicable across authority transfer. Clearing it requires applicable explicit supersession; event-ID order and wall clock cannot make an earlier approval supersede an unobserved refusal. Scoped admission, trust and independence determine review eligibility.

Usage: arc review [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --verdict <VERDICT>
          The conclusion recorded
          
          [possible values: approved, changes-requested, comment-only]

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --json
          Emit the read view as a versioned JSON object

      --body <BODY>
          Inline body text

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read body from file ('-' for stdin)

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --snapshot
          Snapshot the clean change worktree before recording the verdict; --contributors or --solo attribute that patchset. The findings batch and the verdict are validated first, so a refused review records no patchset. The change stays locked from the snapshot until the verdict on that patchset is recorded, so a command acting on the change in between reports the lock busy

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --patchset <PATCHSET>
          Patchset under review, by id or by the revision it recorded. Defaults to the latest — which is what the verdict then claims, whatever the reviewer actually read

      --cause <CAUSE>
          Root cause of requested rework; repeat for a mixed round. Required with `--verdict changes-requested` and refused with any other verdict

          Possible values:
          - brief:                 The patchset faithfully exposed a missing, false, or ambiguous premise
          - executor:              The patchset violated a correct applicable brief
          - integration-staleness: Later target work invalidated a correct brief and implementation

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --findings-json <FINDINGS_JSON>
          Findings batch: a path to a JSON array, or '-' for stdin. Each element is an object with a required `severity` (critical, major, minor, or note) and `summary` (string), and optional `blocking` (bool, default false), `body` (string), and `anchor`. An anchor is an object with a required `path` and optional `side` (base or head, default head), `line_start`, `line_end`, and `context`. An unknown field of a finding or anchor that looks like a misspelling of one it omits (`blocker` for `blocking`, `title` for `summary`, `path` for `anchor`, `line` for `line_start`, `lines` for `line_start` and `line_end`, or one edit away) refuses the batch; any other is ignored with a warning, except a finding's `id`, ignored silently because IDs are assigned by arc. An approval cannot carry a blocking finding

      --relation <RELATION>
          What this verdict does to the verdicts already standing on the change. `supersedes` replaces them; `corroborates` supports one without becoming a second authority, which is what discharging a provisional approval is. Ignored when no verdict stands yet

          Possible values:
          - corroborates: Supports the observed verdicts without replacing them
          - supersedes:   Replaces the observed verdict tips
          
          [default: supersedes]

      --provisional <REASON>
          Say this verdict is owed corroboration, and why. It gates like any other verdict — independence and staleness are unchanged — but the change carries a recorded obligation until somebody else supplies a second judgment, and `arc query --provisional` finds it until they do. Obligation selection follows approval's local-first verdict history; a trusted foreign claim cannot create one beside an authoritative local judgment. Use it when the reviewer's judgment has not been validated: an unproven model, a rushed pass, a reviewer outside their competence. arc never infers this; naming which reviewers are proven would be a routing opinion it does not hold

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that selected this reviewer. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else: arc joins it against no roster and reads no quality from it. Omitted, the review is unrouted

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc verify --help =====
Run a declared gate (or ad hoc command) and record the evidence. Gate evidence only counts at the change's own head, so the command runs in the change's recorded worktree whichever checkout it was typed in; `--attest` records evidence arc did not run. Acceptance executes local head gates even when imported evidence passed, and selects probes from the delivered patchset's bound brief. Baselines run in scratch checkouts

Usage: arc verify [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --all
          Run every gate declared for the change profile

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --parallel
          Run all declared gates concurrently and append evidence in name order

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --skip-green
          Record reuse of passing evidence instead of rerunning the gate that produced it: with --all, evidence green at the current head's tree; with --against, evidence green at the merged tree. Evidence counts for its tree whichever commit it ran on, as it does for readiness, and only the newest evidence readiness counts there is a candidate. Reuse records that tree and requires that evidence to carry the same content key; a gate whose newest evidence failed or has neither tree nor tested_tree is rerun

      --gate <GATE>
          Gate name from .arc/gates.toml

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --command <COMMAND>
          Ad hoc command (recorded, but not a declared gate)

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --probe <PROBE>
          Named acceptance probe declared by a brief

      --brief-version <BRIEF_VERSION>
          Brief version containing --probe (defaults to latest)

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --probe-phase <PROBE_PHASE>
          Acceptance-probe evidence phase (defaults to final). Baseline commands run at the brief's base, in a detached scratch checkout under the configured worktrees directory when HEAD differs. Relative directories resolve against the invoking checkout's root; creation, cleanup and printed commands use the same absolute path. The scratch checkout is removed after the run. Failed creation cleans only this attempt's allocation and administrative entries whose gitdir names its path, preserving unrelated registrations. Cleanup confirms the path is absent from Git's worktree inventory before offering manual recreation commands. Failed cleanup reports the retained path and its removal command. Attested baseline evidence requires --tested-revision equal to the base
          
          [possible values: baseline, final]

      --attest
          Record externally observed evidence without running the command (e.g. a sandboxed executor or another host ran the gate)

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --result <RESULT>
          The attested result; required with --attest, rejected without it
          
          [possible values: pass, fail]

      --tested-revision <TESTED_REVISION>
          Revision actually tested; required with --attest

      --execution-host <EXECUTION_HOST>
          Host or environment that executed the attested command

      --runner <RUNNER>
          Stable identity of the external runner or job

      --environment <IDENTITY>
          Environment identity the attested evidence applies to, as the gate's declared environment probe yields it.
          
          Required with --attest for a gate whose declaration has an environment probe, and rejected for a declaration that has none: an identity is only meaningful against the probe that produces it. Omit it for a run arc observes itself; arc reads the environment from the declared probe

      --note <NOTE>
          Optional note recorded alongside the evidence

      --waive-dirty <REASON>
          Let evidence from a dirty worktree count, saying why.
          
          Dirt is fatal by default: a passing run whose tree no checkout reproduces is recorded and declines to satisfy the gate. The waiver binds the way the evidence binds — to this head alone — so the next commit ends it rather than leaving a standing exemption. It is visible to a reviewer, who is free to disagree with it. A baseline probe requiring a clean scratch checkout refuses this flag before recording; a baseline at HEAD can waive that HEAD.

      --falsified-by <EVENT_ID>
          Earlier failing evidence for this same check that this run answers.
          
          A gate that has only ever passed and a gate watched to fail and then fixed leave the same record. Naming the failure separates them. The event must be a failing verification of the same gate or command on this change; its revision comes from the event itself. Requires --predicted, and is advisory: it changes no gate result, readiness decision, or exit code.
          
          Only this declaration makes a gate `discriminating`. Separately, a pass that follows a failure of the same gate (or command, when unnamed) on this change records the newest such failure as `falsification_inferred`, whether or not this flag is given; that inference decides nothing. Acceptance-probe evidence neither records one nor serves as the failure one names.

      --predicted <REASON>
          Why the check was expected to fail, stated before it ran.
          
          A reason read off the failure afterwards restates the output; one stated beforehand is a claim that could have been wrong, which is what makes the pass that followed mean something. Requires --falsified-by.

      --against <BRANCH>
          Run every required gate against the merge with this branch, not against the change's own head.
          
          A change that is behind its target merges to content neither branch committed, and evidence at the head says nothing about it. This synthesizes that merge, checks it out on its own, runs the declared gates there, and records the result against the merged tree. The scratch checkout is removed whatever the gates do, and is not created at all when no gate is left to run. The evidence is spent as soon as the target moves again, because that is a different merge. With --skip-green, a gate already green at that merged tree records reuse rather than running a second time.
          
          Gate declarations come from this destination's local branch ref, including when it differs from the change's recorded target. A same-named tag cannot select the destination. A textual conflict, including a modify/delete conflict, refuses before any gate runs or evidence is recorded; rebase first.

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc done --help =====
Finish implementation: snapshot, verify all gates, then print check state. A profile with no declared gate records no evidence and prints that no gate is declared instead of a pass.

The caller's live claim moves to `verifying` before the snapshot, and only after every snapshot input is accepted: the attribution, --thread, and each --journal-ref. An input it refuses leaves the claim's stage and its clock as they were.

Usage: arc done [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor

      --journal-ref <FILE>
          Journal artifact this patchset was framed by (repeatable): a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for one in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The link records the reference as given. Its body digest is read from the owning journal when the patchset is recorded, and a name that resolves to no artifact is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path is refused naming the reference that resolves it. Given once or more, only the flagged artifacts are linked, `via: flag`. Omitted, the patchset links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file, a file named by both linked once as `begin`; a default that no longer resolves is left out with a warning on stderr. A rerun at an unchanged head with neither this nor --thread keeps existing links

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --thread <SCHEME:ID>
          External thread this work belongs to, as SCHEME:ID. Arc stores the identifiers and never fetches or resolves them

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>

===== arc rebase --help =====
Replay a change's branch onto its target, then snapshot the new head. The snapshot links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file; a file named by both is linked once as `begin`. An unavailable artifact is omitted with a warning on stderr. Over another actor's live claim, a rebase without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the replayed head is recorded, so a command acting on the change during the replay reports the lock busy. The gates the replayed head owes are named after it is recorded; a failure to evaluate them is a warning, and the rebase still succeeds

Usage: arc rebase [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --verify
          Run every required gate at the replayed head
      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help
          Print help

===== arc env --help =====
Print shell exports for a detected harness session, for `eval`: `eval "$(arc env)"`.

Detection reads the session variable a harness exports for itself — `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` or `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, `OPENCODE_SESSION`, or `PI_SESSION_ID` — and then that harness's own session store for the model, and the effort where the store records one. A harness exports its session id into the processes it starts, so when several harnesses' variables are present it is the nearest ancestor that exported one that owns this process: a pi run inside a Claude Code tool shell reports pi, not the shell's claude. Where the ancestry names no single owner the ambiguity is reported and no harness, session, or model is set, rather than choosing by variable order. The store root is the harness's own override — `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR`, or `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` — before its default under `$HOME`. The store's answer for the exact canonical session id is reported with the exports: an id the store does not hold is uncorroborated; an ambiguous or unreadable lookup is unresolved. Events carry that verdict. Every established harness, session, and session link is exported; absent fields are explicitly unset. The resolved model is a comment and ARC_MODEL is always unset, so each write resolves its own model. Pi re-sets `PI_SESSION_FILE`, `PI_MODEL`, and `PI_REASONING_LEVEL` for every tool call, so those answer in preference to the recording while `PI_SESSION_ID` is the acting session, and a Claude subagent shares its parent's session id. An unfinished child recording leaves the invoking shell's owner ambiguous even if the parent's recording is newer, so no model is named. Claude Code exposes no evidence that distinguishes a subagent's tool shell from its parent's. Completed children permit the parent's recorded model. For a corroborated Claude recording without a model selection, `arc env` and write-time model resolution re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second to allow the model-bearing record to reach disk. Other store answers return immediately. A missing recording and a recording still without a model after the wait each receive an explicit diagnostic. Launch configuration does not establish an observed acting model. Not every harness exports a session variable, and a harness that does may not in every mode. OpenCode v2 exports none and is recognized by `OPENCODE_TERMINAL` or its process ancestry, printing the harness export, unsetting the session and model, and leaving the session as a comment to set by hand.

With nothing to detect at all, or several harnesses and no owner the ancestry can name, it prints the export template as a comment, unsets every identity field, and exits non-zero, which is a report that identity must be set by hand rather than a failure. Every value it emits can be set directly: explicit identity always wins over a detected one.

The model comment includes the newest selection's timestamp and native id, and whether it is inside or before the recording's newest operator turn. An earlier observation may predate an effort change. Leave ARC_MODEL is always unset for write-time resolution; declare a model by hand with ARC_MODEL or --model. Missing boundaries and incomplete head coverage are reported. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Prints the build commit and tracked-tree clean/dirty state as a comment; builds outside Git report unknown.

Usage: arc env [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc integrate --help =====
Explicit --provisional prepares one unsigned no-ff merge into an unchecked staging ref under refs/heads/arc/provisional/<local-replica>/<recorded-target>. Canonical policy, review and gate requirements apply. Publication and closure remain owed to the recipient, defaulting to the known authority holder. Recipient flags route an obligation and grant no authority. Provisional prerequisites satisfy local preparation only; authority publication requires canonical closures. Replacing provisional ancestry requires a content replay, a new patchset and fresh review or explicit debt. Guarded integration of one change, or of a dependency-ordered queue named by several changes or by --tag.

Integration runs where the target is checked out. That checkout's tracked modifications refuse, staged or unstaged. Untracked or ignored paths integration does not write are left in place and named in the report; a path integration would write that is already there without being tracked is refused by name. A head the target already contains closes at the target revision that holds it, without a merge commit. When no checkout holds the target, integration takes over the change's own checkout if it still holds the change branch and leaves it on the target. The integrated revision and closure event are printed before retention cleanup. A retention cleanup failure, including a ref lock or a hook rejecting deletion, warns with the ref prefixes that may still hold pins and does not fail the recorded integration. Style and history constraints are validated under the target and change locks against each authorization policy reading, retaining the original flags and enforcing the destination's declared actor requirement. Missing asserted contribution trailers are advisory only; append them with arc rewrite trailers before integration. Review credit stays in the ledger. Destination declarations are pinned to the transition's observed revision; their actor requirement and agreement with authorization are required. Configuration disagreement refuses before merging. A queue's summary includes style and history refusals and all unattempted members. Known history, style, divergence and lifecycle constraints refuse before queue replay or gate preparation, including invoking-store and destination declared-actor requirements. Each member opens the landing store afresh, so earlier landings govern its append policy. Review refusals name the required local or external reviewer's next decision. Destination names select local branch refs even when a tag has the same name. Ready-to-send reuse requires matching patchset, destination branch and revision, contribution history and authorization. Its event and state retain those coordinates. Fast-forwards bind the ref update to the observed revision and update the checkout with read-tree; checkout failure attempts a guarded ref rollback. Both shapes isolate merge-shaping Git defaults. Transition failure, including checkout takeover, reports ref, HEAD, index and checkout state and preserves local and competing work. Fast-forwards changing initialized submodules refuse before Git mutation regardless of Git submodule ignore settings; use --shape no-ff or integrate manually with submodule updates. Closure publication failure reports whether the event is published, absent or unreadable; inspect publication and durability before retrying.

Usage: arc integrate [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]...

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]...
          Changes to integrate. Several run as a queue, in dependency order, stopping at the first that needs a person. Omit only when selecting with --tag

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --provisional
          Prepare an unsigned merge in this replica's staging namespace; publication remains owed

      --for-replica <FOR_REPLICA>
          Route the obligation to a paired replica; grants no authority

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --for-forge <FOR_FORGE>
          Route the obligation to an opaque forge reference without transport

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --tag <TAG>
          Integrate every change carrying all supplied tags, in dependency order

      --into <INTO>
          Provisional mode selects an unchecked staging branch in the local replica namespace. Its declarations may add gates but cannot weaken canonical policy. Integrate into this branch; its gate, actor, contribution and authorization declarations govern every integration check

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --shape <SHAPE>
          Local integration shape: ff-only advances to the approved head and refuses divergence; no-ff creates a merge commit. Resolution: flag, operator defaults, target project defaults, user config, then ff-only. Project and operator allowed shapes intersect; squash history permits one commit above the member's destination, projected during queue dry runs
          
          [possible values: ff-only, no-ff]

      --message <MESSAGE>
          Merge commit message, requires resolved no-ff shape (defaults to "merge(<slug>): <title>")

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --cleanup
          Delete the merged branch and remove only a clean worktree inside the sandbox, excluding the integration checkout. Arc removes only the checkout instance it created, identified by an owner record in that checkout’s Git administrative directory. The registered checkout must still hold the change branch. An ignored directory outside .arc/ identified as a build artifact by a valid CACHEDIR.TAG, a built-in recognizer (`arc` lists them), or a configured integration.disposable rule's path and marker is eligible for removal only if a scan finds no nested Git repository and encounters no read error. This applies under either discard scope; a worktree containing another repository is never removed. The whole filesystem tree is walked independently of Git status, excluding only the checkout's own .git file at its root, without following symlinks. A recorded worktree path that is itself a symlink keeps the checkout. integration.discard defaults to artifacts; --discard ignored also discards other ignored files and directories outside .arc/. Tracked edits, untracked files and index entries marked skip-worktree or assume-unchanged keep it. Ignored .arc/ entries, including recognized artifacts, keep the checkout under either scope. An outbox holding only the spool's regular .gitignore with "*" and a trailing newline is disposable only when its Git administrative ownership record identifies the file and records successful promotion. Foreign metadata, unrecorded ownership and other outbox content keep the checkout. Kept checkouts retain checked-out branches. Cleanup failures warn after closure and do not fail integration or stop a queue. Overrides integration.cleanup; absent either flag, the resolved style default applies (built-in false)

      --discard <DISCARD>
          Select integration.discard: artifacts (built-in) discards recognized build artifacts; ignored uses the parent checkout's Git ignore rules outside .arc/. A worktree containing another repository is never removed under either scope. Cleanup walks the whole filesystem tree independently of Git status for .git entries and bare repositories (HEAD, objects/, refs/). Repository paths are named. The checkout's own .git file at its root is the only exclusion. The walk never follows symlinks; any read error keeps the checkout. Tracked edits, untracked files and index entries marked skip-worktree or assume-unchanged keep the checkout. Ignored .arc/ entries, including recognized artifacts, keep it under either scope; only owned, emptied outbox metadata is exempt. Ownership checks apply under either scope. Cleanup uses cleanup, discard and disposable values selected before the merge; a change's policy edits do not govern its own cleanup. Implies --cleanup; overrides configured discard scope for single changes and queues
          
          [possible values: artifacts, ignored]

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --no-cleanup
          Keep the merged branch and worktree, overriding integration.cleanup

      --dry-run
          Report what would happen without merging, closing, or writing. Queues stop at known blockers before projection; future gates, replay and checkout guards are conditional. Style, history, approval, danger and provenance resolve against each member's projected destination. Exact-patchset review vetoes stop even an unresolved projection. After a divergent merge or replay, later destination ancestry, merged policy, style, history and authorization remain conditional. Prerequisites follow supersession successors through projected closures, rejecting missing successors and cycles

      --json
          With --dry-run, print the plan as `arc-integration-plan/1` JSON: the authorization basis the integration event would record, the target revision it was decided at, the approved head, and the tree the integration would ship, and its shape. The same decision governs the dry run and execution; a refusal prints the blocker on stderr and nothing on stdout. Provisional retries identify a completed preparation through reuse_event_id and reuse_commit, with merge_commit false. With provisional execution, emit `arc-provisional-transition/1` with observed refs, event publication, retained intent and recovery command. A later refusal includes any debt written or reused earlier in the invocation. Applied intent recovery precedes eligibility checks for a fresh preparation. Unapplied retries enforce current readiness and the saved basis. Explicit destination or recipient conflicts refuse with the exact retry parameters. One change only

      --expect-basis <FILE>
          Compare the fresh decision with a plan an earlier `--dry-run --json` printed. The fresh readiness evaluation alone decides; the file never changes the decision. When the approved head, the target revision, the gate or policy declarations, or the approval moved since, a refusal adds one line naming what moved. A permitted integration proceeds with a warning naming what moved. Provisional plans also compare recipient and prerequisite references. An unchanged basis adds nothing. One change only

      --debt <REASON>
          Integrate without an independent verdict, recording the review this change still owes. One change only: the reason binds to one patchset, so it has nothing to say about a queue. It stands in for a verdict nobody recorded — and for a self-approval policy would reject — in the same invocation. It never overrules a reviewer who read this patchset and asked for changes: that is a verdict, not a missing one. The obligation survives closure and `arc query --debt` finds it; discharge it with `arc audit`. A refused integration keeps the debt it declared, and a retry with the same reason reuses it. Divergence observed under the target and change locks refuses before declaration. External Git moving the target after that observation can retain debt

      --kind <DEBT_KIND>
          What kind of deficit the debt records. Omitted, arc derives it from the ledger; a declared kind wins, because arc cannot tell a merge resolution from a repair

          Possible values:
          - nothing-read:            No verdict on any patchset of the change
          - merge-resolution-unread: An approved patchset, then a merge or rebase resolution nobody read
          - repair-unread:           An approved patchset, then authored work nobody read
          - contributor-only:        Verdicts on the shipped patchset, all of them from its contributors
          - independent-review:      A read by somebody independent, which nobody supplied. Also what an obligation recorded before kinds existed means

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Arc exits 11 when --shape ff-only encounters a diverged target; run arc rebase <change> --verify and provide fresh review or declared debt. When --into selects a different destination, arc rebase cannot select it: run git rebase <destination> in the change checkout, then arc snapshot <change> --verify and obtain fresh review or debt. Arc exits 17 when a paired replica does not hold integration authority; the refusal names the holder or an offer in flight.

===== arc debt --help =====
Record a review obligation this change carries but has not discharged

Usage: arc debt [OPTIONS] --reason <REASON> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change that owes the review

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --reason <REASON>
          What review is owed, and why it could not run. The debt already in force, declared again unchanged, is reused rather than recorded twice

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --kind <KIND>
          What kind of deficit this records. Omitted, arc derives it from the ledger; a declared kind wins, because arc cannot tell a merge resolution from a repair

          Possible values:
          - nothing-read:            No verdict on any patchset of the change
          - merge-resolution-unread: An approved patchset, then a merge or rebase resolution nobody read
          - repair-unread:           An approved patchset, then authored work nobody read
          - contributor-only:        Verdicts on the shipped patchset, all of them from its contributors
          - independent-review:      A read by somebody independent, which nobody supplied. Also what an obligation recorded before kinds existed means

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help

===== arc close --help =====
Close a change without arc performing the merge

The change and closure event are printed before retention cleanup. A retention cleanup failure, including a ref lock or a hook rejecting deletion, warns with the ref prefixes that may still hold pins; the recorded closure succeeds.

Usage: arc close [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change to close

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --assert-integrated <ASSERT_INTEGRATED>
          Assert an integration arc did not perform, at this revision. Carries no authorization: arc did not guard this merge

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --patchset <PATCHSET>
          The patchset that was integrated (defaults to the latest)

      --into <INTO>
          The branch it was integrated into (defaults to the target branch)

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --target-before <TARGET_BEFORE>
          Assert where the target stood before, naming an ancestor of the asserted revision regardless of its parent count. If omitted, a merge head supplies its first parent; other heads record no base

      --abandoned
          Close as abandoned: the work stopped and nothing was merged

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker

      --superseded <SUPERSEDED>

      --role <ROLE>

          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

===== arc history --help =====
Record or withdraw rewrite maps, and resolve recorded revisions

Usage: arc history [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  withdraw  Withdraw a recorded history-rewritten map by event ID; other event types are refused. Ref moves are not undone; output names recorded moves, or says when the map has no ref-move information. Withdrawals travel with maps in bundles and are honoured by importing replicas
  rewrite   Record a rewrite performed elsewhere, with its commit map. Active maps must agree; a withdrawn map does not constrain its replacement
  resolve   Show where a recorded revision ended up through active maps. Withdrawn maps are ignored; exits 2 when no recorded rewrite moved it Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  help      Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

===== arc candidate --help =====
Register, show, list, judge, and retire candidates: alternative answers to a brief, recorded without opening a change

Usage: arc candidate [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  register  Register a candidate: a tree answering one version of a change's brief, with who produced it and where it came from
  show      Show one candidate: tree, contract, producers, parents, adoptions, episodes, judgements, retirement, whether its pin holds the tree, evaluations, selections naming it (promoted, superseded, or not promoted), promotion refs no `candidate-promoted` event records, and every registration sharing its tree. `--json` emits `arc-candidate/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  list      List registrations as `show` renders them, grouping every tree more than one registration names under `shared_trees`. `--json` emits `arc-candidate/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  judge     Record a judgement of a candidate by its declarant: rejected, or superseded by another registration. A judgement changes no registration and selects nothing; `unknown-candidate` refuses an id not registered
  retire    Delete `refs/arc/candidate/<id>` and record `candidate-retired`, only when no root reaches the candidate. Roots are selections and promotions, and a root reaches what its candidate's parents and adoptions carry; when one does, exit 1 naming it, writing nothing. The registration stands. Retiring a retired candidate says so and exits 0. arc never retires a candidate on its own
  verify    Evaluate a candidate: run the required gates of its brief's change, as that change's target declares them, against the candidate's tree
  select    Validate a named selection and, when every ground holds, record it and promote the chosen candidate into its brief's change
  promote   Promote a recorded selection, or finish one an interrupted run left
  help      Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc policy --help =====
Read or write policy kept with this repository's local Arc ledger

Usage: arc policy [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  show   Show effective policy, publishing enrollments, review trust, gate environment probes, and each declaring file. Replica grants are operator-only. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  path   Print the operator policy file path for this repository Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  write  Replace the operator policy file with TOML from a file or stdin. Replacement shares the admission publication lock. Publishing principals and trusted review origins in [replica] are operator-only. Provisional preparation settings may also be declared by canonical project policy. Privileged roles require explicit actor allowlists. Style uses [defaults.change] and [defaults.integration]; constraints use [integration] shapes and history. Malformed tables and invalid known keys refuse with file and key before writing
  help   Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

===== arc doctor --help =====
Check the append-only ledger for malformed or stale state (read-only) Reports the build commit and tracked-tree clean/dirty state in text and arc-doctor/6 JSON; builds outside Git report unknown. Unfinished provisional transitions report their merge, refs, event state, retained intent and exact recovery command as problems.

Exits 0 when the ledger is clean or carries only advice, and 1 when it reports a problem. A ledger that cannot be read at all also exits 1, with the error on stderr and no report; a usage error exits 2. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

===== arc workspace --help =====
Aggregate changes, inboxes, or backlog across every known project

Usage: arc workspace [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  list       Per-repo open-change rows across the data_root Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  inbox      The inbox rollup for every registered project, including the count of open actionable filings untouched for more than 14 days. Plain reads record no activity; active claims suppress staleness. Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  inventory  Every artifact across the selected stores, each with why it is where it is: present, terminal, archived, or superseded. It accepts the same `--under`/`--here`/`--global` scope, reports the same collection manifest, exits 16 on a partial collection, and is versioned `arc-workspace-inventory/2`. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  backlog    Ledger and journal backlog across every project, ranked by what is blocked on a decision rather than on work. Debt rows carry their verbatim `reason` and optional `patchset_id`. A partial collection keeps the projects it did read, names each failure, and exits 16 Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  report     The backlog classified by named rules: each artifact's status, the section it belongs to, and the facts that deserve attention. Review-owed rows carry the effective `kind`, declared `reason`, nullable `patchset_id`, `declared_at`, and `declared_by`. Versioned `arc-workspace-report/3`. With `--previous`, tallies carry their earlier values when both collections are complete, new rows are marked when their project was observed both times, and every artifact that left the backlog is listed with its recorded reason. The baseline must have the same schema and scope. Every failed component read, including the report's ledger read, enters the collection manifest and makes the command exit 16. Attention facts, listed below by rule, never change the exit code. The report writes nothing. Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. A separate provisional integration section carries recipient, age, refusal and reconciliation evidence. Unfinished transitions have a separate section with refs, event publication and recovery commands
  help       Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
