===== arc catchup --help =====
Orient a session: ledger queue, journal backlog, live lanes, and recent sessions across harnesses. Recent activity without filed artifacts or a naming handoff/conclusion is marked unfiled. Filing counts include direct writes and published transition successors, with import and spool provenance. Stale filings name the `journal filed` query; plain reads record no activity. Project memories precede global memory titles with their ages in days; bodies are read on demand with journal show --global. An unavailable journal carries its own warning, including global_memory_error or project_journal_error in JSON; readable sources remain in the output. Each recording backend is queried once with an unrestricted candidate window. Backend scan bounds and failures appear in session_discovery_errors; readable rows remain ranked by recorded activity. Pending transitions carry a diagnostic and do not count as filings or naming handoffs/conclusions. Text warns when the running build differs from HEAD in an arc-ledger checkout: older for an ancestor, differs otherwise, with dirty builds identified. Other projects and unknown build revisions have no warning. Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output Provisional integration obligations have a separate section and do not count as review debt

Usage: arc catchup [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --sessions <SESSIONS>          Number of recent sessions, selected after ranking every discovered candidate by its last recorded activity rather than file modification time. Backend scan bounds can hide sessions that would rank inside this limit [default: 5]
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --limit <LIMIT>                Cap the changes listed per ledger bucket; the journal queue is always rendered in full, since finding it is the point [default: 10]
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc fr --help =====
File a feature request in the project journal: the discoverable alias for `arc journal note --kind feature-request`. Read the queue back with `arc journal open` or `arc journal list --kind feature-request`

Usage: arc fr [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>  Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --title <TITLE>                Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug
      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --no-scaffold                  Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --planned-by <JSON>            Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal --help =====
Cross-harness project and operator journals over plain Markdown. Project reads also resolve from their journal directory; project writes name the checkout they need. --global works from any directory

Usage: arc journal [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  dir              Print the resolved journal directory (creates nothing)
  doctor           Check the journal for malformed or stale state (read-only)
  spool            List journal writes parked in the repository-local outbox, for a caller that could not reach the journal directory. Unrecorded cleanup ownership keeps the checkout after promotion
  note             Write a timestamped artifact and append its journal line
  feature-request  File a proposal nobody is building yet
  todo             Record work waiting for a session
  handoff          Hand an unfinished thread to the next session
  plan             Record a plan before it becomes work
  conclusion       Record what a piece of work concluded
  decision         Record a settled decision, which is what resolves a discussion
  incident         Record something that went wrong in the running of work, not in the code, so a decision's revisit trigger can fire on evidence
  memory           Record a durable fact, surfaced by `catchup` every session. --global selects the operator journal; bodies are read with show
  later            Park a proposal that is real but not now
  review           Record a review performed outside the ledger
  log              Append a log-only journal line (no artifact file is created). Prepared appends and promotions recover before writing; unprepared torn trailing lines refuse with a command preserving their bytes. With `--source --item-key --outcome no-action` the line is the standing answer that this source item needs no work, so a later scan of the same session is answered without an artifact entering any queue
  source-attach    Record a second recorded session as a source of an existing artifact. One item may be evidenced by several sessions; this appends the extra coordinate as an event and never touches the artifact's body
  source           What one recorded session already produced here: every item key filed against it, its disposition, the artifact when one exists, and the events behind it. This is what makes a repeated scan of the same endings cheap — an item already filed, discharged, or judged to need no action answers for itself instead of being distilled again Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  filed            Filed artifacts grouped by their writing session, with source references, changes that took them up, and consumption outcomes (arc-journal-filed/1). Direct writes and published transition successors retain their original writers, including imported and spool-promoted artifacts. Successors name the artifact they supersede. An intent without a readable successor body carrying its supersedes link is omitted; journal doctor reports pending transitions. Open actionable items are stale after the threshold of recorded inactivity; active claims suppress staleness. Plain reads record no activity. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  position         Add a position block to an artifact and emit a typed `position` event. `--stance` writes the `Position: for | against | amend` line above the body; without it, the body can provide that line itself. Use `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>` to argue under one branch of an open question instead of unconditionally
  verified         Record that an artifact was checked against the project's source at the current anchor revision. The revision is omitted when the anchor has no Git head; the stamp appears on the open queue until the artifact is consumed or the anchor moves
  question         Pose a question on a discussion that only a person can settle, and emit a typed `question` event. Placement is the design: `opening` is answered before any position is filed, so everyone argues from the same premise; `closing` is answered once the argument is in. There is no mid-argument placement — a question that blocks halfway makes the caller watch a run they delegated. Argue a closing question on both sides first, with `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>`. Pose one with `arc journal question <file> --placement opening|closing --option A --option B --body-file -`
  checkpoint       Append a work checkpoint to an artifact this session has claimed: where the work stands, what comes next, and what a successor should read. Requires a live claim held by this identity. Later checkpoints in one claim are ordinary progress and replace its earlier ones; a correction names what it replaces with `--supersedes`, and the flag repeats so a takeover converges several continuations at once
  scaffolds        List the scaffolds a write can add to its body, and print one before using it. A journal artifact is append-only, so choosing between `--scaffold`, a kind's default, and `--no-scaffold` blind makes a wrong guess permanent Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  questions        Every open question, across the journal. arc records what agents cannot settle alone; it does not ask. This is the view an agent reads to raise the question through its own harness prompt — file, id, placement, the options to offer, and which branches were already argued — and `answer` is where the reply comes back. `--json` carries the same, for building the prompt without parsing text Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  answer           Settle an open question by choosing one of its options. Each answer append carries its own block digest and precise timestamp, so answers to a reopened question remain distinct during revision exchange. Branches that lost stay in the file: a branch argued and not taken is the only record that the alternative was explored rather than never considered. Use `arc journal answer <file> --question <id> --option <chosen> --body-file -`
  suggest          Suggest one of a question's options without settling it. A suggestion carries the suggesting identity and its reason, shows beside the question in every question view, and is input to a decision: it never closes the question and never counts toward the stance tally. Use `arc journal suggest <file> --question <id> --option <opt> --body-file -`
  delivered        Record that a caller delivered an open question to somebody. arc keeps the fact; it sends nothing, holds no messaging credentials, and infers no delivery from prose. Without it a question queue cannot separate prompting work still to do from an answer merely pending, so every question view reports `delivery: unknown|unasked|delivered|answered`. Deliveries accumulate — asking twice records twice and erases nothing — and a question waiting on one named delegate accepts only that delegate. Use `arc journal delivered <file> --question <id> --to person|anyone|delegate:<name> [--handle <opaque>]`
  correct          Replace one field of one recorded entry without rewriting it. The entry stays in the artifact as it was filed and a `### Correction` block records the replacement; derived views read the corrected value. `--target` names the entry: `artifact`, a position ID, a question ID, or `answer:<question id>`. A consumed artifact accepts corrections — it is closed to new work, but a wrong record stays wrong
  retract          Withdraw a recorded entry while leaving it visible. A retracted position leaves the stance tally and the branches argued under a question; a retracted answer reopens the question it settled. The block stays in the artifact, because an argument withdrawn is a different record from an argument never made. A consumed artifact accepts retractions
  reattribute      Repair the recorded authorship of one artifact's creation event, in place. A maintenance operation the operator selects: ordinary journal mutation stays append-only and no correction block is appended. Only the named fields and dependent model evidence of the one creation (`note`) event change; every other record keeps its bytes, and the previous log is kept as `events.jsonl.bak`. Repair and every event append share an event-write lock across the read and replacement. Other event kinds are refused because their identity carries contribution, decision, or continuation that provenance repair must not rewrite, and so is a creation event recording an on-behalf-of subject. `--dry-run` names the exact record and every other record carrying the replaced identity, and writes nothing. A model replacement is a flag declaration compared with the retained observation; changing harness or session clears that observation because it belongs to the replaced session. A pending exchange refuses repair; finish it with `arc journal import <bundle>` first
  events           Dump the journal event log as newline-delimited JSON. A trailing line without its newline refuses with a recovery diagnostic. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  catchup          Newest-first listing of artifacts plus the journal tail (read-only) Text warns when the running build revision differs from an arc checkout. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  memories         List live shared project memories newest first Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  open             List actionable artifacts in primary, later, and feature-request tiers Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  list             List live artifacts (hot journal dir) newest first, optionally filtered by kind (read-only) Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  inventory        Project one observation of journal artifacts and their orthogonal lifecycle facts. Hot storage is the default; `--archived` is explicit. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  show             Print one artifact's raw Markdown body to stdout (read-only) Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  latest           Resolve the newest artifact filed under one topic and print it, so a workflow that appends continuations under a stable topic can read its own tail back without reproducing arc's ordering rules. The topic must match exactly; a similarly prefixed topic never matches. The hot journal wins over the cold archive whenever both hold a match. Inside one timestamp second a transition's terminal successor is preferred, and otherwise the order the event log recorded the artifacts in decides; a numeric filename suffix is never an ordering of its own. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  discussion       Derived summary of a discussion: stance tally, participants, replies, age, and resolution with a resolver-participation flag (read-only). The tally counts `Position: for | against | amend` lines inside `### Position` blocks, and flags blocks that state no stance. Reports each open question with the positions argued under every option, so a branch nobody explored is visible before the question is answered. Resolve a discussion with `journal consume --outcome done --decision <file>` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  rebind           Adopt an existing journal for this project, recording the move. Refuses when both journals hold content: two histories are separable only while they are apart
  stamp            Print the current UTC timestamp in the journal house format (read-only) Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  lane             Manage advisory session work lanes
  consume          Mark an artifact consumed so it leaves the `open` queue
  transition       Change a live artifact's workflow kind without rewriting history: a typed successor is written, linked back with `supersedes`, and the source is retired as superseded. The safe manual sequence, performed as one guarded operation rather than hand-composed by every caller. Promotion to a code change stays with `begin --from-journal`, which is implementation authorization and never a kind conversion
  promote          Promote a live project memory into the operator journal. Requires --global. Both journals record the relation; the project memory is consumed as superseded and its historical body is retained. Both journals must have settled exchanges. An interrupted promotion is completed before subsequent writes or by retrying the same command. Complete staged bodies publish exclusively; success requires body and event replay verification. Changed bodies or a different global journal refuse recovery. Open claims require release-claim
  archive          Move artifacts to the cold sibling archive without deleting history
  unarchive        Restore one archived artifact to hot storage
  export           Export selected journal artifacts as a versioned bundle for another replica. --global exports the operator journal without Git pairing. The bundle carries each artifact body, the events recorded about it, its body digest, the exporting replica, and every artifact the selection references by filename. Exports arc-journal-bundle/2 Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  import           Import arc-journal-bundle/2 or /1 from another paired replica. --global accepts only operator journal bundles, without Git pairing. Prefix revisions retain the longer body. Concurrent appended sections merge by event identity, ordered by timestamp then ID. Answers to a reopened question retain distinct append identities. Edits to the base or a shared block refuse the entire bundle, naming both digests and the first differing block. Storage tiers must agree. Events retain their provenance; a repeated bundle changes nothing. Interrupted writes retain a prepared transaction; an import completes it before comparing revisions. Every journal write, including provenance repair and spool promotion, refuses a pending import and names its bundle digest and `arc journal import <bundle>` remedy. Reads stay allowed. Recovery refuses to overwrite unexpected body or event-log changes. --dry-run requires a completed exchange
  help             Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

Create the fork worktree and journal its marker

The registered checkout must retain its allocated Git administrative directory after checkout hooks run. A changed identity refuses the marker and keeps all worktrees. Post-checkout receives Git's creation arguments (null old revision, HEAD, 1). Population or hook failure retains the branch and worktree and reports fork adoption and discard commands without recording a marker.

          Branch to fork from; omitted, the current branch, or the repository's integration branch when standing on a fork

A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. ===== arc fork adopt --help =====
Journal a marker for a hand-made fork worktree

Usage: arc fork adopt [OPTIONS] <SLUG>

Arguments:
  <SLUG>  The fork slug: the name the fork is recorded and retired under

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --branch <BRANCH>              The branch this fork is; defaults to fork/<slug>, and an adopted fork keeps whatever name it has. An open change's branch is refused: a marker over it would make the change unintegrable
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --intent <INTENT>              What the fork is for, recorded in the marker
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc rewrite sign --help =====
Recreate every commit from --from to the branch head so it is signed by one key, moving the refs and recording the map

Usage: arc rewrite sign [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --key <KEY>                    The key to sign with; Git's configured signing key by default
      --from <FROM>                  Oldest commit to recreate, inclusive. Defaults to the oldest not signed by the key in target..head on a tracked change branch, or in the whole history outside a change
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --include-shared               Permit recreating commits reachable from the target or another local branch, and default to the whole history. Without this flag shared commits are refused before any moves, including --dry-run
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --dry-run                      Print the map the rewrite would record and stop
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --no-sign                      Recreate the commits without signing them, for a repository with no signing key
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --retag                        Recreate each annotated tag whose target was rewritten on the commit that replaced it, carrying its message, tagger and date, and signing it with the key the commits are signed with; --no-sign leaves it unsigned
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc rewrite trailers --help =====
Edit the trailers of every commit message from --from to the branch head, recreating only the commits that change and what sits on them

      --drop <KEY>                   Remove every trailer with this key, matched without case; repeat for more than one
      --append <LINE>                Add this `Key: value` line to the trailer block where it is not already there; repeat for more than one
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --from <FROM>                  Oldest commit whose trailers are edited; required, since a trailer edit has nothing to infer a range from
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --key <KEY>                    The key to sign a recreated commit with; Git's configured signing key by default
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --dry-run                      Print the map the rewrite would record and stop
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --no-sign                      Recreate the edited commits without signing them, for a repository with no signing key
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --retag                        Recreate each annotated tag whose target was rewritten on the commit that replaced it, carrying its message, tagger and date, and signing it with the key the commits are signed with; --no-sign leaves it unsigned
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc candidate register --help =====
Register a candidate: a tree answering one version of a change's brief, with who produced it and where it came from.

Records `candidate-registered` as a repository event and pins the tree at `refs/arc/candidate/<id>`. Registering opens no change and creates no patchset. Prints `candidate: <id>`. Two registrations of one tree are two candidates that share storage and nothing else.

Refused, naming the rule and writing nothing: `duplicate-candidate` for an id already registered; `no-producers` with no --producer; `unknown-tree` for a tree or commit the object store does not hold; `unknown-brief` for a reference that resolves to no recorded brief; `unknown-parent` or `unknown-adopted` for an id not registered; `parent-other-contract` for a parent answering another change or brief version; `adoption-drops-producer`, naming them, when the producers omit anyone along the adopted registration's parent chain; `unknown-episode` for a claim not recorded on the brief's change.

Usage: arc candidate register [OPTIONS] --tree <TREE> --brief <BRIEF>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --tree <TREE>
          The content: a tree, or a commit, which is recorded as its tree

      --brief <BRIEF>
          The contract answered, `<change>[@<brief-event>]`; the latest brief version when no event is named. Recorded with a `sha256:` digest of the brief body

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --producer <PRODUCERS>
          Who produced the content, repeated for each; at least one

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --parent <PARENTS>
          A registration this one continues, such as the candidate a repair repairs; it must answer the same brief version. Repeatable

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --adopts <ADOPTS>
          A registration whose content this one carries into its own contract; the producers must include every producer along its parent chain. Repeatable

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --episode <EPISODES>
          A claim id recorded on the brief's change that the work ran under. Repeatable

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --id <ID>
          The candidate id; generated from the change's slug when omitted

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate retire --help =====
Delete `refs/arc/candidate/<id>` and record `candidate-retired`, only when no root reaches the candidate. Roots are selections and promotions, and a root reaches what its candidate's parents and adoptions carry; when one does, exit 1 naming it, writing nothing. The registration stands. Retiring a retired candidate says so and exits 0. arc never retires a candidate on its own

Usage: arc candidate retire [OPTIONS] <ID>

Arguments:
  <ID>  Candidate id

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. ===== arc candidate verify --help =====
Evaluate a candidate: run the required gates of its brief's change, as that change's target declares them, against the candidate's tree.

The tree is checked out in a scratch worktree, removed afterwards, and each gate's environment probe runs there first. Each gate records `candidate-verified` as a repository event keyed by the tree, with the gate, its command, timeout, and environment probe as declared when it ran, the environment identity the probe yielded, and the result; `evaluation: <event>` names it for `select --evaluation`. Exits 0 when every gate run passes and 1 otherwise. Refused: `unknown-candidate`; `unknown-gate` for a --gate the change is not required to pass; `target-unreadable` when the target cannot be read

Usage: arc candidate verify [OPTIONS] <ID>

Arguments:
  <ID>
          Candidate id

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --gate <GATE>
          Run only this required gate

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread

          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

===== arc candidate select --help =====
Validate a named selection and, when every ground holds, record it and promote the chosen candidate into its brief's change.

arc never chooses: the proposal names the registration, the destination, the target head decided against, and the evaluations relied on. Every failing ground is reported, one line each led by its code, exit 1 and nothing written: `reuse-policy-undeclared` without `[candidates] evaluation_reuse`; `unknown-candidate`, and `candidate-retired` for a registration whose pin was released; `destination-other-contract` when the registration answers another change's brief; `destination-closed`; `target-moved` when --target is not the target head now; `environment-unobserved` for a gate whose probe yields no identity at the shipped tree. Each required gate needs a named, passing evaluation at the shipped tree (the chosen registration's), under the declaration in force and in the environment observed now, from the chosen registration or, under `matching-coordinates`, from any registration whose tree, declaration, and environment match; otherwise, per named evaluation, `unrecorded`, `other-registration` (under `never`), `other-tree`, `other-declaration`, `environment-unrecorded`, `environment-other`, or `failed`, and `no-evaluation` for a gate none is named for. Each `--must-read` of the registration's brief version needs a tool read (`arc context read`) on the registration or one along its parent chain, by an episode one of them cites, at the required version (an artifact's body digest; a file's blob, inferred or by the digest of its bytes over the range read), covering the required extent; otherwise `partial`, `unknown-coverage`, `only-declared`, `only-supplied` (the contract's own plan or opening artifact), or `not-read`. Reads through an adoption do not count.

A permitted selection records `candidate-selected` with its basis: the chosen registration, destination head, target, the evaluation and read meeting each requirement, the reuse policy, the contributors (the producers along the parent chain), the selector, and the rationale. A selection replacing an unpromoted one for the same destination names it as superseded. The promotion then runs as `arc candidate promote` describes; its refusal leaves the selection standing without one

Usage: arc candidate select [OPTIONS] --chosen <CHOSEN> --into <INTO> --target <TARGET> --rationale <RATIONALE>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --chosen <CHOSEN>
          The registration chosen

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --into <INTO>
          The destination change: the change whose brief it answers

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --target <TARGET>
          The destination's target head the choice was made against

      --evaluation <EVALUATIONS>
          A `candidate-verified` event relied on for a required gate (repeatable)

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --rationale <RATIONALE>
          Why this candidate: text, or `@<file>`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate promote --help =====
Promote a recorded selection, or finish one an interrupted run left.

The promotion holds the destination's transition lock and the repository-events lock. It re-reads the destination head and target and refuses `basis-moved` when either differs from the selection's basis, recording nothing: a basis is never reused, so select again. It refuses a checkout of the destination branch with tracked modifications, or one holding untracked or ignored paths the update would write. It commits the shipped tree onto the destination head with the selector as committer, keeps it at `refs/arc/candidate-promotion/<candidate>/<selection>`, moves the branch from the head it read (a concurrent move stops the promotion there), updates the checkout, records a patchset with the selection's contributors and a candidate link, then `candidate-promoted`.

Given a promotion ref with no `candidate-promoted` event, it records the patchset and the promotion when the branch already points at the ref's commit, and otherwise deletes the ref and says so. A promoted selection is a no-op that says so; a superseded one is refused

Usage: arc candidate promote [OPTIONS] <SELECTION>

Arguments:
  <SELECTION>
          The `candidate-selected` event

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc context read --help =====
Record a read record: a tool's record that a call succeeded and returned bytes for a path and range, within one episode of work on a subject.

The read's version is the `sha256:` of the bytes the tool returned. Coverage is the range the tool recorded: `--lines`, `--whole`, or, with neither, `unknown`, which never counts as whole. A failed call is never a read. With `--at`, the returned bytes are compared with the path's blob at that revision over the same range (lines with their terminators; the whole blob for `whole` or `unknown`): when they are equal, the blob is recorded as an inferred locator labelled `content-matches-revision`; when they differ, as under uncommitted edits or a tool that decorates what it returns, or when the revision holds no such path, no blob is recorded and the read stands on its digest alone. A path directly inside a journal directory or its cold archive is also recorded as that journal artifact, with the artifact body's digest at recording, which is compared with the read's and never assumed equal: by file name in this project's journal, and as the qualified `<journal-dir>::<file>` in another project's.

`--from-tapes <file>` reads a tool record document instead: `tapes-events/9` (`tapes events <session> --json`) or `tapes-session/14` (the `.json` of a `tapes export` bundle, which carries the same events). Every tool call with a `read` member becomes a read record: its `event_id` is the record id, `read.path` the path, `read.lines` a line range or `read.whole` whole coverage (neither is `unknown`), and `read.sha256` the digest. A call with no stable event id, no path, or no digest, one that failed (`read.succeeded` false, or its own or its paired result's `status` `error` or `failed`), and a record id already recorded on the subject are each skipped with one printed line naming the event and the reason. arc reads only the file it is handed; it opens no harness store and runs no program. `read.sha256` is the digest of the text the tool returned, recorded as tapes reports it and never normalized: a tool that returns decorated text, such as Claude Code's line-numbered `Read`, has a digest that equals no file's bytes, so `content-matches-revision` does not fire for it.

A change subject's reads are events on that change; a candidate's are repository events, so every bundle carries them. Refused, writing nothing: `unknown-subject`; `unknown-episode` for a claim not recorded on the subject's change (a candidate's brief change); `duplicate-record` for a record id already recorded on the subject; `malformed-digest`; `unknown-revision` for an `--at` naming no commit.

Usage: arc context read [OPTIONS] --subject <SUBJECT> --episode <EPISODE> <--record <RECORD>|--from-tapes <FILE>>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --subject <SUBJECT>
          The change or candidate id the read belongs to

      --episode <EPISODE>
          The claim id the read happened under

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --record <RECORD>
          The tool record's stable identifier

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --path <PATH>
          The path the tool recorded

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --digest <DIGEST>
          `sha256:` over the bytes the tool returned

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --lines <LINES>
          The line range the tool recorded, `<from>-<to>`, one-based and inclusive

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --whole
          The tool recorded the whole file

      --at <AT>
          Compare the returned bytes with the path's blob at this revision

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --from-tapes <FILE>
          A `tapes-events/9` or `tapes-session/14` document to take the reads from

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc context declare --help =====
Record a declaration: the declarant's claim that a subject cites, relies on, or considers a path (optionally at a revision) or a journal artifact.

A declaration is attributed to its declarant, never checked against what was read, and never becomes a read. `--citation` points it at a read record on the same subject; one that resolves to no recorded read is refused as `unknown-citation`, writing nothing.

Usage: arc context declare [OPTIONS] --subject <SUBJECT> <--cites|--relies-on|--considers> <--path <PATH>|--artifact <ARTIFACT>>

A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. ===== arc context capture --help =====
Record what the provider reported for the retention of a read record's recording (`tapes capture <session> --json` is that report).

A capture report is a claim about retention, attributed to its declarant; the latest report for a record stands. `arc explain` reads a read whose recording has no standing `pinned` report as `at risk`. The report joins the ledger of the one subject holding the record; `--subject` names it when several do. Refused, writing nothing: `unknown-record` for a record not recorded as a read; `ambiguous-record` when several subjects hold it and none is named.

Usage: arc context capture [OPTIONS] --record <RECORD> <--pinned|--unpinned>

          The provider reported the recording pinned

          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

          The provider reported the recording not pinned

          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. ===== arc run dispatch --help =====
Record that a caller dispatched a run through a resolved route. Exactly one subject is named: rounds are numbered within it, so a run against nothing belongs to no sequence

Usage: arc run dispatch [OPTIONS] --route <ROUTE> --worktree <WORKTREE>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                 Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --route <ROUTE>                 Resolved route used for dispatch
      --harness <HARNESS>             Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --worktree <WORKTREE>           Worktree path given to the run
      --change <change_flag>          Change the run is against
      --session <SESSION>             Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --fork <FORK>                   Fork slug the run is against, for work outside the lifecycle
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>   Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                 Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --range <RANGE>                 Commit range the run is against, as <base>..<head>, for work with no ledger change
      --brief-event <BRIEF_EVENT_ID>  Brief event given to the run, when one exists
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>   Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --note <NOTE>                   Free-text dispatch note
      --role <ROLE>                   Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>             Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                          Print help

===== arc run end --help =====
Record the terminal outcome of a dispatched run, with what the round reviewed, raised, and deliberately left

Usage: arc run end [OPTIONS] --outcome <OUTCOME> <DISPATCH_EVENT_ID>

Arguments:
  <DISPATCH_EVENT_ID>  RunDispatched event ID being closed

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                  Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --outcome <OUTCOME>              Terminal outcome supplied by the caller [possible values: completed, refused-on-premise, stopped, unknown]
      --harness <HARNESS>              Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --reviewed-head <REVIEWED_HEAD>  Revision the round reviewed
      --raised-json <RAISED_JSON>      Findings raised for repair: a JSON array of objects with a `summary` and an optional `severity`, from a file or '-' for stdin
      --session <SESSION>              Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --deferred-json <DEFERRED_JSON>  Findings deferred: the same array, each object additionally carrying a required `why` and an optional `id` (one is minted when absent)
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>    Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --collects <COLLECTS>            Deferral this round takes up, by ID; repeat for each. The deferral must be open on the same subject
      --model <MODEL>                  Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --note <NOTE>                    Free-text ending note
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>    Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                    Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>           Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>              Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc policy write --help =====
Replace the operator policy file with TOML from a file or stdin. Replacement shares the admission publication lock. Publishing principals and trusted review origins in [replica] are operator-only. Provisional preparation settings may also be declared by canonical project policy. Privileged roles require explicit actor allowlists. Style uses [defaults.change] and [defaults.integration]; constraints use [integration] shapes and history. Malformed tables and invalid known keys refuse with file and key before writing

Usage: arc policy write [OPTIONS] --body-file <FILE>

Options:

===== arc hooks install --help =====
Install the arc hook scripts into this repository's hooks dir

Both hooks are advisory and always exit 0. `post-commit` says when the commit staled an approval or landed on a closed change's branch; `prepare-commit-msg` appends an `Arc-Change: <change-id>` trailer on an open change's branch when the message lacks one. The hooks directory honours `core.hooksPath`

Usage: arc hooks install [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --force
          Replace a foreign hook, saving it as <hook>.pre-arc

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

===== arc workspace backlog --help =====
Ledger and journal backlog across every project, ranked by what is blocked on a decision rather than on work. Debt rows carry their verbatim `reason` and optional `patchset_id`. A partial collection keeps the projects it did read, names each failure, and exits 16 Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc workspace backlog [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --since <SINCE>
          Count only journal items filed at or after this journal stamp (20260101T000000Z) or RFC 3339 timestamp, so the tiers read as arrivals rather than as what is outstanding. Changes awaiting a verdict and debt are always reported in full: a blocker matters more the longer it has been one

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --items
          Name every actionable artifact, instead of counting them

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --under <PATH>
          Report only projects whose canonical anchor is beneath this path

      --here
          Report only projects beneath the current directory

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --global
          Report every registered project, the default when no scope is set

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --unreachable
          Name every unreachable journal, including temporary and scratch anchors

      --rank-by <RANK_BY>
          Rank projects by this fact, highest first. Every row carries all three facts unchanged, so the choice only sets the order

          Possible values:
          - blocking:     Verdicts owed plus decisions waiting on a person: the project's blocking facts. Coverage obligations are not blocking and are not counted here
          - availability: Primary work waiting to be picked up
          - coverage:     Coverage obligations owed on shipped work
          
          [default: blocking]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc workspace report --help =====
The backlog classified by named rules: each artifact's status, the section it belongs to, and the facts that deserve attention. Review-owed rows carry the effective `kind`, declared `reason`, nullable `patchset_id`, `declared_at`, and `declared_by`. Versioned `arc-workspace-report/3`. With `--previous`, tallies carry their earlier values when both collections are complete, new rows are marked when their project was observed both times, and every artifact that left the backlog is listed with its recorded reason. The baseline must have the same schema and scope. Every failed component read, including the report's ledger read, enters the collection manifest and makes the command exit 16. Attention facts, listed below by rule, never change the exit code. The report writes nothing. Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. A separate provisional integration section carries recipient, age, refusal and reconciliation evidence. Unfinished transitions have a separate section with refs, event publication and recovery commands

Usage: arc workspace report [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --under <PATH>                 Report only projects whose canonical anchor is beneath this path
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --here                         Report only projects beneath the current directory
      --global                       Report every registered project, the default when no scope is set
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --previous <FILE>              An earlier `arc workspace report --json` to compare against
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

Attention rules:
  delivered-unconsumed      A delivered artifact: every promotion closed, one integrated, and nothing consumed it
  stale-question            A decision question open longer than 7 days
  stale-handoff             A handoff unresolved longer than 14 days
  stale-claim               A claim that lapsed and can be reclaimed
  stale-no-patchset         A change open longer than 7 days with no patchset
  shared-plan-slice         Two or more open changes whose in-force briefs share a plan and slice, or a body digest
  worktree-outlives-change  A closed change whose separate worktree is still on disk
  debt-grew                 Review owed in a project rose since the previous report
  collection-failed         A component read failed; the collection is partial
  unreachable-anchor        A registered project whose anchor is gone
  unreachable-scratch       Registered journals whose temporary or scratch anchors are gone, folded into one fact
  candidates-unjudged       A promoted candidate whose sibling registrations, answering the same brief version, nobody has judged

===== arc journal dir --help =====
Print the resolved journal directory (creates nothing)

Resolution takes the first source that answers: ARC_JOURNAL_DIR; the longest `[journals] dirs` prefix of the repository root, or of the directory outside a repository; `<ai-home>/journals/<repo-path-slug>` for a Git repository; then a default-root journal whose `bindings.jsonl` records this directory, or the one this directory's slug names. A prefix covering repositories shadows their Git journals Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

===== arc journal doctor --help =====
Check the journal for malformed or stale state (read-only)

Exits 0 when the journal is clean or carries only advice, and 1 when it reports a problem. A journal that cannot be read at all also exits 1, with the error on stderr and no report; a usage error exits 2. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

===== arc journal note --help =====
Write a timestamped artifact and append its journal line

Usage: arc journal note [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>  Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --title <TITLE>                Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug
      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --no-scaffold                  Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --planned-by <JSON>            Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
      --kind <KIND>                  Artifact kind (closed set). Defaults to `note`; every other kind has its own subcommand above, which is the same write said plainly. `discussion` is the exception and has none: a discussion is argued and read far more often than created, so its verbs are `position`, `question`, `answer` and the `discussion` summary, and `--kind discussion` stays the way to open one. It also brings the `discussion` scaffold unless told otherwise, which puts the body under `## The question` and ends on `## Positions`, so every position appended follows that heading [default: note] [possible values: note, memory, plan, handoff, review, conclusion, todo, later, discussion, decision, incident, feature-request]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal feature-request --help =====
File a proposal nobody is building yet

Usage: arc journal feature-request [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>  Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --title <TITLE>                Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug
      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --no-scaffold                  Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --planned-by <JSON>            Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help
