===== arc journal log --help =====
Append a log-only journal line (no artifact file is created). Prepared appends and promotions recover before writing; unprepared torn trailing lines refuse with a command preserving their bytes. With `--source --item-key --outcome no-action` the line is the standing answer that this source item needs no work, so a later scan of the same session is answered without an artifact entering any queue

Usage: arc journal log [OPTIONS] <TOPIC> <MESSAGE>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>    Kebab-case topic slug
  <MESSAGE>  Free-text journal message

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --outcome <OUTCOME>            Record this source item as needing no work at all. `no-action` is the only outcome a log line can carry: every other disposition belongs to an artifact [possible values: no-action]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout

What one recorded session already produced here: every item key filed against it, its disposition, the artifact when one exists, and the events behind it.       --harness <HARNESS>            The recording's harness, as the source reference recorded it
      --session <SESSION>            The native session id, as the source reference recorded it
      --item-key <KEY>               Report only this item key
      --json                         Emit structured JSON instead of text
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. ===== arc journal filed --help =====
Filed artifacts grouped by their writing session, with source references, changes that took them up, and consumption outcomes (arc-journal-filed/1). Direct writes and published transition successors retain their original writers, including imported and spool-promoted artifacts. Successors name the artifact they supersede. An intent without a readable successor body carrying its supersedes link is omitted; journal doctor reports pending transitions. Open actionable items are stale after the threshold of recorded inactivity; active claims suppress staleness. Plain reads record no activity. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal filed [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --session <SESSION>            Filter by an exact writing or source session id
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --stale <STALE>                Mark open actionable items stale beyond this many days [default: 14]
      --json                         Emit structured JSON instead of text
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal position --help =====
Add a position block to an artifact and emit a typed `position` event. `--stance` writes the `Position: for | against | amend` line above the body; without it, the body can provide that line itself. Use `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>` to argue under one branch of an open question instead of unconditionally

Usage: arc journal position [OPTIONS] --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --ref <REFERENCE>              Position or item this answers: a position ID, legacy timestamp, or item slug. Quote the claim answered on the line below the stance
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (the position argument, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Position` heading)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --stance <STANCE>              Stance to write above the body and record on the typed event. Omit this when the body already opens with its own `Position:` line [possible values: for, against, amend]
      --question <QUESTION>          Argue under one option of an open question, rather than unconditionally. Pass the question ID; `--option` names the branch
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --option <OPTION>              The option this position argues under; requires `--question`
      --archived                     Explicitly append to an unresolved discussion in cold storage
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal question --help =====
Pose a question on a discussion that only a person can settle, and emit a typed `question` event. Placement is the design: `opening` is answered before any position is filed, so everyone argues from the same premise; `closing` is answered once the argument is in. There is no mid-argument placement — a question that blocks halfway makes the caller watch a run they delegated. Argue a closing question on both sides first, with `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>`. Pose one with `arc journal question <file> --placement opening|closing --option A --option B --body-file -`

Usage: arc journal question [OPTIONS] --placement <PLACEMENT> --option <OPTIONS> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --placement <PLACEMENT>        When it is answered: `opening` settles a premise before any position is filed; `closing` settles a choice the argument raised. There is deliberately no mid-argument placement [possible values: opening, closing]
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --option <OPTIONS>             An answer the question offers; repeat for each. Two or more
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (what the question asks, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Question` heading)
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --settle-by <SETTLE_BY>        Who may settle it: `person` (the default, what the prose always meant), `anyone`, or `delegate` with --delegate <name>. A question marks what this session should not settle alone, not that no model may answer — an operator who delegated the call names the delegate [possible values: person, anyone, delegate]
      --delegate <DELEGATE>          Settle-by delegate, as the name allowed to answer (use with --settle-by delegate)
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal checkpoint --help =====
Append a work checkpoint to an artifact this session has claimed: where the work stands, what comes next, and what a successor should read. Requires a live claim held by this identity. Later checkpoints in one claim are ordinary progress and replace its earlier ones; a correction names what it replaces with `--supersedes`, and the flag repeats so a takeover converges several continuations at once

Usage: arc journal checkpoint [OPTIONS] --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (direction, remaining work, and rejected approaches, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Work checkpoint` heading)
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --next <NEXT>                  The action a successor should take next
      --gate <GATE>                  The command that says whether the work is good
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --blocker <BLOCKER>            What the work waits on: `change:<id>`, or free text for anything outside arc
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --supersedes <SUPERSEDES>      The checkpoint this one corrects; repeat to converge several in one takeover. Every target stays readable
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal scaffolds --help =====
List the scaffolds a write can add to its body, and print one before using it. A journal artifact is append-only, so choosing between `--scaffold`, a kind's default, and `--no-scaffold` blind makes a wrong guess permanent Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal scaffolds [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --show <NAME>                  Print this scaffold's body instead of listing the names
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal questions --help =====
Every open question, across the journal. arc records what agents cannot settle alone; it does not ask. This is the view an agent reads to raise the question through its own harness prompt — file, id, placement, the options to offer, and which branches were already argued — and `answer` is where the reply comes back. `--json` carries the same, for building the prompt without parsing text Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal questions [OPTIONS]

===== arc journal answer --help =====
Settle an open question by choosing one of its options. Each answer append carries its own block digest and precise timestamp, so answers to a reopened question remain distinct during revision exchange. Branches that lost stay in the file: a branch argued and not taken is the only record that the alternative was explored rather than never considered. Use `arc journal answer <file> --question <id> --option <chosen> --body-file -`

Usage: arc journal answer [OPTIONS] --question <QUESTION> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --question <QUESTION>          The question being settled
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --option <OPTION>              The option chosen; must be one the question offered. A typo is refused rather than silently recorded as an answer nobody offered
      --other <ANSWER>               An answer none of the offered options expressed, in the answerer's own words. Recorded as settling the question and as evidence the option set was inadequate, which `arc journal questions` reports — a menu the answerer had to step outside of was framed wrong, and that is worth knowing before the next one is posed
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (why, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Answer` heading)
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal suggest --help =====
Suggest one of a question's options without settling it. A suggestion carries the suggesting identity and its reason, shows beside the question in every question view, and is input to a decision: it never closes the question and never counts toward the stance tally. Use `arc journal suggest <file> --question <id> --option <opt> --body-file -`

Usage: arc journal suggest [OPTIONS] --question <QUESTION> --option <OPTION> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --question <QUESTION>          The open question the suggestion is input to
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --option <OPTION>              The option suggested; must be one the question offered
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (the reason, recorded verbatim on the suggestion event)
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc journal delivered --help =====
Record that a caller delivered an open question to somebody. arc keeps the fact; it sends nothing, holds no messaging credentials, and infers no delivery from prose. Without it a question queue cannot separate prompting work still to do from an answer merely pending, so every question view reports `delivery: unknown|unasked|delivered|answered`. Deliveries accumulate — asking twice records twice and erases nothing — and a question waiting on one named delegate accepts only that delegate. Use `arc journal delivered <file> --question <id> --to person|anyone|delegate:<name> [--handle <opaque>]`

Usage: arc journal delivered [OPTIONS] --question <QUESTION> --to <RECIPIENT> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --question <QUESTION>          The question that was delivered
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --to <RECIPIENT>               Who was asked: `person`, `anyone`, or `delegate:<name>`
      --handle <OPAQUE>              Whatever opaque identifier the delivery system handed back — a thread id, a ticket, a message URL. Recorded verbatim so the delivery can be found again outside arc, and never resolved
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal correct --help =====
Replace one field of one recorded entry without rewriting it. The entry stays in the artifact as it was filed and a `### Correction` block records the replacement; derived views read the corrected value. `--target` names the entry: `artifact`, a position ID, a question ID, or `answer:<question id>`. A consumed artifact accepts corrections — it is closed to new work, but a wrong record stays wrong

Usage: arc journal correct [OPTIONS] --target <TARGET> --field <FIELD> --value <VALUE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --target <TARGET>              The entry to correct: `artifact`, a position ID, a question ID, or `answer:<question id>`
      --field <FIELD>                The field to replace. Which fields exist depends on the target: `title` on the artifact; `stance`, `option`, `ref`, `actor`, and `model` on a position; `option`, `actor`, and `model` on an answer; `actor` and `model` on a question [possible values: stance, option, actor, model, ref, title, planners]
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --value <VALUE>                What the field should have said
      --note <NOTE>                  Why the recorded value was wrong
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal retract --help =====
Withdraw a recorded entry while leaving it visible. A retracted position leaves the stance tally and the branches argued under a question; a retracted answer reopens the question it settled. The block stays in the artifact, because an argument withdrawn is a different record from an argument never made. A consumed artifact accepts retractions

Usage: arc journal retract [OPTIONS] --target <TARGET> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --target <TARGET>              The entry to withdraw: a position ID or `answer:<question id>`
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (why it is withdrawn, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Retraction` heading)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal reattribute --help =====
Repair the recorded authorship of one artifact's creation event, in place. A maintenance operation the operator selects: ordinary journal mutation stays append-only and no correction block is appended. Only the named fields and dependent model evidence of the one creation (`note`) event change; every other record keeps its bytes, and the previous log is kept as `events.jsonl.bak`. Repair and every event append share an event-write lock across the read and replacement. Other event kinds are refused because their identity carries contribution, decision, or continuation that provenance repair must not rewrite, and so is a creation event recording an on-behalf-of subject. `--dry-run` names the exact record and every other record carrying the replaced identity, and writes nothing. A model replacement is a flag declaration compared with the retained observation; changing harness or session clears that observation because it belongs to the replaced session. A pending exchange refuses repair; finish it with `arc journal import <bundle>` first

Usage: arc journal reattribute [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename this project's journal or its cold archive holds (a name, not a path). A file only another known journal holds, or its `<journal-dir>::<file>` reference, is refused naming that journal and its project, where the repair runs

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --event <EVENT>                The event kind to repair; only the creation event (`note`) is supported and anything else is refused by name [default: note]
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --set-actor <ACTOR>            Replacement actor; absent leaves the recorded actor alone
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --set-harness <HARNESS>        Replacement harness; absent leaves the recorded harness alone
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --set-session <SESSION>        Replacement session; absent leaves the recorded session alone
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --set-model <MODEL>            Replacement model; absent leaves the recorded model alone
      --dry-run                      Name the record and the other records carrying the replaced identity, then write nothing
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal latest --help =====
Resolve the newest artifact filed under one topic and print it, so a workflow that appends continuations under a stable topic can read its own tail back without reproducing arc's ordering rules. The topic must match exactly; a similarly prefixed topic never matches. The hot journal wins over the cold archive whenever both hold a match. Inside one timestamp second a transition's terminal successor is preferred, and otherwise the order the event log recorded the artifacts in decides; a numeric filename suffix is never an ordering of its own. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal latest [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>  Kebab-case topic slug, matched exactly

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --kind <KIND>                  Restrict to one artifact kind, including kinds no longer written
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --json                         Emit the resolved identity alongside the body as JSON
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal discussion --help =====
Derived summary of a discussion: stance tally, participants, replies, age, and resolution with a resolver-participation flag (read-only). The tally counts `Position: for | against | amend` lines inside `### Position` blocks, and flags blocks that state no stance. Reports each open question with the positions argued under every option, so a branch nobody explored is visible before the question is answered. Resolve a discussion with `journal consume --outcome done --decision <file>` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal discussion [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Discussion artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --json                         Emit structured JSON instead of text
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

===== arc journal consume --help =====
Mark an artifact consumed so it leaves the `open` queue

          Optional context appended to the journal line

      --session <SESSION>

      --drop-questions
          Consume anyway, leaving an unanswered question undecided. Say in `--note` where the question went, or it goes nowhere
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --acknowledge-claim <ACKNOWLEDGE_CLAIM>
          Acknowledge one open claim on the artifact by ID (repeatable). Consumption ends the work somebody else is doing, so it names every claim it ends rather than discovering them afterwards
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes.           Print help

===== arc journal transition --help =====
Change a live artifact's workflow kind without rewriting history: a typed successor is written, linked back with `supersedes`, and the source is retired as superseded. The safe manual sequence, performed as one guarded operation rather than hand-composed by every caller. Promotion to a code change stays with `begin --from-journal`, which is implementation authorization and never a kind conversion

Usage: arc journal transition [OPTIONS] --to <TO> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --to <TO>
          The successor's kind [possible values: note, memory, plan, handoff, review, conclusion, todo, later, discussion, decision, incident, feature-request]
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Body source for the successor: a file path, or '-' for stdin. Omitted, the successor carries the source body verbatim under a heading naming what changed
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --title <TITLE>
          Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug
      --reason <REASON>
          Why the kind is changing, recorded in the transition event
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --dry-run
          Print the successor and the lifecycle effects without writing anything
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --acknowledge-claim <ACKNOWLEDGE_CLAIM>
          Acknowledge one open claim on the source artifact by ID (repeatable). A kind change retires the source, ending work in progress on it
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --planned-by <PLANNED_BY>
          Assert an additional material planner on a plan successor (repeatable): a one-line JSON object in the shape `arc journal plan --help` states for --planned-by
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --no-planner
          Preserve unknown authorship on a plan successor
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help
          Print help

===== arc journal promote --help =====
Promote a live project memory into the operator journal. Requires --global. Both journals record the relation; the project memory is consumed as superseded and its historical body is retained. Both journals must have settled exchanges. An interrupted promotion is completed before subsequent writes or by retrying the same command. Complete staged bodies publish exclusively; success requires body and event replay verification. Changed bodies or a different global journal refuse recovery. Open claims require release-claim

Usage: arc journal promote [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Project memory filename (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal export --help =====
Export selected journal artifacts as a versioned bundle for another replica. --global exports the operator journal without Git pairing. The bundle carries each artifact body, the events recorded about it, its body digest, the exporting replica, and every artifact the selection references by filename. Exports arc-journal-bundle/2 Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc journal export [OPTIONS] --output <OUTPUT> <FILES>...

Arguments:
  <FILES>...  Artifact filenames inside the journal dir (names, not paths); the dependency closure of what they reference travels with them

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --output <OUTPUT>              Output file ('-' for stdout)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal import --help =====
Import arc-journal-bundle/2 or /1 from another paired replica. --global accepts only operator journal bundles, without Git pairing. Prefix revisions retain the longer body. Concurrent appended sections merge by event identity, ordered by timestamp then ID. Answers to a reopened question retain distinct append identities. Edits to the base or a shared block refuse the entire bundle, naming both digests and the first differing block. Storage tiers must agree. Events retain their provenance; a repeated bundle changes nothing. Interrupted writes retain a prepared transaction; an import completes it before comparing revisions. Every journal write, including provenance repair and spool promotion, refuses a pending import and names its bundle digest and `arc journal import <bundle>` remedy. Reads stay allowed. Recovery refuses to overwrite unexpected body or event-log changes. --dry-run requires a completed exchange

Usage: arc journal import [OPTIONS] <INPUT>

Arguments:
  <INPUT>  Input file ('-' for stdin)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --dry-run                      Validate and report without writing artifacts, events, or a receipt
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc replica provisional resolve --help =====
Resolve late delivery against this change's accepting integration. Requires current authority. Forge obligations require asserted integration with the exact external reference

Usage: arc replica provisional resolve [OPTIONS] --integration <INTEGRATION> --because <BECAUSE> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>  Change whose provisional delivery is being reconciled

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared
      --integration <INTEGRATION>    Accepting change-integrated or integration-asserted event ID
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --provisional <PROVISIONAL>    Obligation event IDs; omitted, resolve every matching outstanding obligation
      --because <BECAUSE>            Reason for explicit reconciliation; never silently disposes of a refusal
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc journal lane open --help =====
Open or replace an advisory work lane

A lane's owner is the declared harness and session together, so both must be given. Opening replaces only that owner's own previous lane; another harness presenting the same session string keeps its lane.

Usage: arc journal lane open [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>
          Kebab-case topic slug naming the lane

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --scope <SCOPE>
          Journal topics this lane covers, so `journal open` can annotate the items another session is already holding

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --ttl <TTL>
          How long the lane stays live without activity (e.g. 30m, 2h)
          
          [default: 2h]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --status <STATUS>
          What the lane is currently doing, shown beside it

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --status-file <STATUS_FILE>
          Read the lane status from a file ('-' for stdin)

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal lane close --help =====
Close a lane

A live lane closes only for the owning harness and session; a stale one closes for anyone with `--outcome expired`.

Usage: arc journal lane close [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>
          The lane to close

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --outcome <OUTCOME>
          How the lane ended
          
          [default: done]
          [possible values: done, handoff, abandoned, expired]

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --note <NOTE>
          Free-text detail recorded with the closure

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
